## Working rules (Ben, 2026-11-21)

- **Work from this folder.** Remmy and Vane both work from `collaboration-framework/` — documents are written here, examined from here, and committed from here.
- **Date everything added.** Every file added to this folder carries its date (in the filename or a date line in the document).
- **Keep this folder updated as we go** (Ben, 2026-11-21 11:57 MST) — this index and the folder's contents are brought current in the same round as any change, never after.

## Structure (created 2026-11-21, shelf commit 916b3153)

The template layout stands inside this folder: `docs/` (vision.md; requirements/ — the SPEC SHEET lives here, current Revision 5 (approved 2026-11-21); architecture/, api/, decisions/ — ADR-001 records the substrate decision), `src/` (frontend / backend / shared), `tests/` (unit / integration / e2e / fixtures), `scripts/`, `infra/`, `.github/workflows/`, plus README.md, CONTRIBUTING.md, and .env.example. Plan Stage 2 (build the core) opened with Ben's design ruling of 2026-11-21; directories not yet built into still hold placeholders. The first build piece — the substrate carry-in at the ADR-002 pin (shelf commit 87c9ec08) — was carried into `src/fabric/` and `src/observer/` on 2026-11-22 (item 26).

220. **RESEARCH NOTE — HOW OTHERS HANDLE TIMEOUTS/DEADLINES (2026-11-23, at Ben's ask):** `RESEARCH-TIMEOUT-PATTERNS-2026-11-23.md` — a sourced comparison (gRPC/Google SRE deadline propagation; Temporal's four timeouts + heartbeat; Step Functions; DBOS deadline inheritance; LangGraph per-node run/idle timeouts; OpenAI Agents per-attempt timeout; Vercel AI SDK total/step/chunk timeouts; Mastra total+step; MCP progress-reset timers; OTel GenAI stage timing). Headlines: one propagated deadline with remaining-time inheritance is the established distributed-systems pattern (stacked independent timeouts are the documented failure mode); the heartbeat/idle-timeout family kills on silence, not duration — the mature cousin of Ben's span-as-observer; **no LLM framework runs a pure run-level deadline with per-call durations merely observed** — the field pairs a total with enforced sub-caps or progress signals; stage timing (TTFT + per-token) is standard observability, with provider internals unexposed (our "unknown" labels match). Discussion input for the attempt-5 configuration examination; changes nothing settled.

235. **ATTEMPT 6 EXAMINED — SETTLEMENT: STRUCTURED, AGENT-DIRECTED RETRIEVAL (2026-11-23):** `VANE-ATTEMPT6-EXAMINATION-RETRIEVAL-SETTLEMENT-2026-11-23.md` (PB pb-mwtxu0k5-2k-7utr). Attempt 6's facts accepted (provider rejection; the route fast, the envelope refused). **The remedy is structured retrieval, not package slimming** — slimming treats the symptom and preserves the pattern. Her formulation of the defect: the worker chooses which files to read but **not how much it retrieves**; a read returns the whole document and the result enters the next request in full — "that is not the pull model Ben described." The worker must be able to discover material without its contents, inspect structure, request a section or bounded range, search within permitted material, and decide successive retrievals from what it has learned — with **search results and metadata under the same boundary as content** (no side door around the manifest). **Attempt 7 is NOT released under the unchanged run scope**; a narrow retrieval scope is required (discovery, bounded results, successive requests, manifest enforcement on every operation, provenance, no whole-document injection, whole-collection-avoidance tests). The three sources and the assignment stay intact. A first implementation of Ben's direction — **not** the A4 build. Her conclusion: the worker shouldn't need to swallow the library to find one useful paragraph.

The director's direction and the examiner's settlement (INDEX 235) converge on the same remedy in the same minutes, independently: structured, agent-directed retrieval, pulled forward ahead of attempt 7. Three retrieval tools in a new `tool-registry-v3` (both registry copies; v1/v2 frozen): `sandbox.list` (structure map, no contents), `sandbox.read_range` (a section or line range under a **hard 12,000-byte result ceiling** — oversized requests refused with the bound named, never silently truncated), `sandbox.search` (labelled passages within permitted material, same ceiling). `sandbox.read` refuses oversized results on the same terms, so whole-document injection becomes impossible through any door. Every operation — metadata included — resolves against the sealed grants and evidence boundary. Results enter the package as exactly what was pulled, with provenance labels; retrieval outcomes record parameters + the returned content's sha256 and size. 239. **RETRIEVAL SCOPE REV 2 — RESUBMITTED (2026-11-23):** the four corrections folded in. (1) **Aggregate ceiling: 36,000 bytes** of retrieved content per package (a named design bound), accounted from the record (settled retrieval outcomes since the previous package) and **enforced pre-execution at the doorway bounds check** — a request is admitted only if aggregate-so-far + the per-result ceiling ≤ the aggregate ceiling; refusals are recorded with the bound and remaining allowance named; the checker recomputes the accounting. 242. **RETRIEVAL SCOPE REV 3 EXAMINED — NOT PASSED; THREE IMPLEMENTATION-CRITICAL AMBIGUITIES (2026-11-23):** `VANE-RETRIEVAL-SCOPE-REV3-EXAMINATION-2026-11-23.md` (PB pb-mwty7iby-2o-h4ve). The Rev 3 corrections are present; the direction is right. **Ambiguities:** (1) the aggregate accounting reads *settled* outcomes — requests admitted concurrently against the same total could collectively overrun it; required: **serialized dispatch** (each outcome settles before the next admission check) or atomic reservation, with **the checker verifying the accounting including outstanding/abandoned state**; (2) the boundary text contradicts the scratch grant — **two authority paths** (source: grant + boundary membership; scratch: its own sealed grant + designated path, never source evidence), **doorway-enforced**, the distinction preserved in the recorded authority provenance the checker verifies; (3) inward UTF-8 adjustment at both ends can **strand a character in neither slice** — a deterministic assignment rule and an R3 **multibyte fixture** proving byte-for-byte reconstruction are required. 243. **RETRIEVAL SCOPE REV 4 — RESUBMITTED (2026-11-23):** the three ambiguities closed. (1) **Serialized admission**: requests from one model response are dispatched one at a time, each outcome settling before the next admission check; an **uncertain** retrieval counts its full ceiling against the window; stall discipline closes accounting before any stall, so nothing dangles; the checker verifies each window's total (settled sizes + uncertain at ceiling) ≤ the aggregate ceiling. (2) **Two authority paths**, doorway-enforced: sources require grant + sealed boundary membership; `scratch/` requires its separately sealed scratch grant alone and exempts no other path; every outcome records its admitting path and the checker fails any outcome whose basis mismatches its path's scope. (3) **Straddle assignment**: served start advances to the next character boundary (a start-straddling character belongs to the previous slice), served end **extends** to the next boundary (an end-straddling character belongs to this slice) — adjacent windows tile exactly; the maximum requestable window is the ceiling minus 3 so a served slice never exceeds the ceiling; R3 gains a multibyte fixture with a character deliberately straddling the boundary, reconstructed byte-for-byte.

It would answer Vane's frequency question (INDEX 266 Q1) by design rather than by argument. **Status: Ben's idea, banked; NOT yet carried to Vane and NOT a build direction — Remmy's closing question (record as his proposal and carry it to Vane as the director's contribution to her three questions) awaits his word.**

270. **⭐⭐ VANE EXAMINES BEN'S NATIVE-MODE STAGE PROPOSAL — SUPPORTED IN PRINCIPLE, WITH THE DISCIPLINE SPECIFIED (2026-11-23):** `VANE-NATIVE-MODE-STAGE-EXAMINATION-2026-11-23.md` (PB pb-mwu1s3w6-31-aogk). "Ben has found a much stronger direction here" — the framework adapts its interface to the assembled worker **without adapting its authority boundary**. Her specification: the probe as a recorded assembly-stage operation (after identity, before judgement/packing); a synthetic probe case with a small fixed catalogue of harmless test tools and **no real filesystem, shell, network, credentials, shelf or side effects**; raw responses captured, **calls observed, never dispatched**; bounded calls/tokens/time; outcome classes text-contract / native-catalogue / environment-tool-attempt plus mixed, malformed, refusal, provider failure; **requested vs provider-accepted vs executed distinguished** (execution never happens in the probe). **Frequency tightened:** consistency claims need a small bounded **versioned trial set** reported as N-of-M counts; results never inherited across model/provider/config changes. The translation boundary is non-negotiable (native call → adapter translation → doorway → adjudication → dispatch → execution → outcome); environment capabilities are never granted for being asked for. The probe establishes **only** observed interaction behaviour — not identity, competence, trustworthiness or permission; the examiner weighs its evidence, the mode never selects itself. A scope must prove the listed points incl. **fail-closed on missing/malformed/inconclusive probes** and **one negative acceptance case** (an ungranted native request in work, refused without execution). **Recommendation: develop the scope — a bounded addition to A1's lifecycle, no implementation or attempt implied.**

(2) LOOP_ITERATION does not show the call was **entered** — the pre-call exception needs an independently visible call boundary, mutually exclusive with it. **Rev 5 (same file):** the helper's raw path records a **phase observation** (`pre_response` / `response_received` + status); `no_response` survives only as `connection_failed_pre_response` on that evidence; the timeout class is withdrawn to `unresolved` (`timeout_after_send`); a mid-read body failure is **captured partial** (status + partial bytes + `body_complete: false`, completeness false); absence/ambiguity of phase evidence defaults to `unresolved`. And a loop-owned **CALL_ENTERED** event before the callable is invoked makes the three histories (pre-call terminal / entered-no-response / returned) distinguishable from the record alone, the pre-call terminals mutually exclusive with it. CALL_ENTERED's semantics are stated bindingly, with four doorway rules (loop actor only, existing iteration, at most once, mutually exclusive with pre-call terminals; post-call events refused while a disposition is owed). 342. **PACED REPETITION — RELEASE WITHHELD: THE PACING CLOCK MUST BE MONOTONIC (2026-11-24):** `VANE-NATIVE-OFFER-PACED-RELEASE-EXAMINATION-2026-11-24.md` (PB pb-mwuka33g-13-kwfs). The pacing correction is otherwise well targeted. The one blocker: the runner's default clock is wall-clock `time.time`, used for both elapsed calculation and start recording — a forward wall-clock jump could fake the 65-second interval; a backward jump over-waits; the fake-clock witnesses exercise neither. Required: a **monotonic clock for elapsed/pacing**, wall-clock retained for chronology and distinguished from the measurement, **monotonic start offsets bound into the trial hash**, and witnesses that wall-clock jumps cannot shorten the interval and that the runner does not proceed before the interval has actually elapsed. Correction dispatched; the five-trial release remains unspent.
343. **RESEARCH PAUSE — NATIVE TOOL-USE RESEARCH DIGEST (2026-11-24):** `NATIVE-TOOL-USE-RESEARCH-2026-11-24.md` (the full sourced report, from the research run Ben ordered when he paused the loop). Headlines: the unoffered-tool reach is a **documented, benchmarked failure mode** (tool hallucination; *The Reasoning Trap* — reasoning/agentic training proportionally increases it; tool-calling is prediction, not registry lookup); **the exact refusal is attested on the same model family + provider** by independent practitioners (tools as prompt text → "it tried to press its own button instead of writing it as plain text"); gpt-oss's Harmony format makes tool calls a native channel (commentary) with browser/python as its trained built-ins — `container.exec` is a real ChatGPT tool but **no source shows gpt-oss was trained with it** (the name's origin is speculation; the reach is the affordance); a 14-model study found text tools better overall **but gpt-oss-120b specifically worse with text (−6.4pp)** — "taking the schema away removes the thing they were optimised for"; the providers' own prescribed pattern is native offering + host-side checked execution (OpenAI's flow, Groq's local tool calling); Groq enforces `tool_choice=none` by wholesale rejection, and catching unoffered calls in our own resolver (an error result the model can learn from) is the documented improvement over a provider 400. "Unnatural" as a literal claim is unmeasured; its evidenced form (distribution mismatch for this pinned model) is well supported, not by a controlled study of this exact setup. Carried to Vane as the loop's shared insight; the paced run stays paused.

Monotonic clock end to end (re-measure-and-re-wait loop; wall clock kept for chronology only, fields split and both hash-bound: `request_started_wall_at` + `request_start_monotonic_offset_seconds`); four new witnesses (wall +600 s forward jump ignored; −3600 s backward jump ignored; early-return sleeper re-waits, 28 sleeps; artifact reconstruction + hash binding). Suite now **35 checks ALL PASS (Remmy re-ran and counted)**; comparison still byte-identical (`cmp`); no live calls. 345. **VANE'S RESPONSE TO THE RESEARCH — ASSESSMENT MATERIALLY CHANGED; NATIVE-FIRST FAVOURED, AUTHORITY UNMOVED (2026-11-24):** `VANE-RESEARCH-RESPONSE-NATIVE-FIRST-2026-11-24.md` (PB pb-mwukkf1b-14-cbpl). Central conclusion: native tool expression is the model's normal request language; the framework retains complete authority over what happens next. (1) The text contract was fighting this model's training — text-only is now, in her assessment, a poor default **for this model and task shape** (not universally); the no-controlled-study qualification stands. (2) **Native presentation ≠ native authority**: the call is a structured request; resolution against the sealed catalogue, validation, grant/bounds/policy checks, doorway-only dispatch, full recording, results by call ID — "the provider's tool schema is an interface description, not an authority document." (3) Unoffered calls should become an **explicit doorway decision**: provider returns the call, our resolver rejects + records, then returns a narrow factual error — never dispatched for being returned; `disable_tool_validation` worth investigating within its limits. (4) `failed_generation` salvage stays a fallback, never the foundation, and only ever via translation into a typed request through the same doorway. (5) Two questions kept separate — **Q1 empirical** (provider reliably returns native calls for granted tools under the exact configuration) and **Q2 architectural** (adapter translates every call into the existing contract with no second authority path); she favours a native-first adapter with a closed-world resolver, not yet a build. The paced repetition's release requirement stands, unauthorised; she examines the clock correction next, then decides whether that experiment is still the right next measurement. Her line of record: **"adapt the framework's interface to the model, not the framework's authority to the model."** No build, scope, or request authorised.
346. **OPENCLAW RESEARCH (2026-11-24):** `OPENCLAW-RESEARCH-2026-11-24.md` (full sourced report, Ben's ask alongside INDEX 343). OpenClaw (Steinberger; Nov 2025 as Clawdbot → Moltbot → OpenClaw; foundation-run since July 2026) is a self-hosted always-on agent runtime, not an SDK. Findings of record for the framework: it uses **providers' native tool calling**, with tools **policy-filtered before the model sees them** (8-layer pipeline, deny wins) and three distinct controls — tool policy / sandbox / **host-side exec approvals** (stricter-of-two, bound to canonical command+path, allow-once default, fail-closed) — independent corroboration of the native-first + doorway shape. **Its defaults invert its hardening** (sandbox off, host exec full/no-ask, agent-to-agent allow-all, secrets unencrypted at rest); the security record is heavy (CVSS 8.8 one-click RCE; tens of thousands of exposed gateways; ~12% of audited marketplace skills malicious in one audit; the canonical case of a prompt-carried "don't act until I tell you" lost to context compaction, 200+ emails deleted — approval in a prompt is not a gate, the negative proof of our record/doorway principle). Multi-agent is delegation (spawn/send, depth caps, per-agent workspaces, credentials unshared) with **no work-state/approval-authority separation** — approval exists only for host exec; hierarchies are prompt conventions. Adopt-list: policy-filtered offering, host-side approval interlock details, spawn discipline, audit-as-feature. Hedges: late-2026 adoption figures are secondary estimates; several CVE tallies conflict by counting method.
347. **PACED RELEASE — CLOCK PASSES; ONE MEASUREMENT-BOUNDARY DEFECT REMAINS (2026-11-24):** `VANE-PACED-CLOCK-EXAMINATION-MEASUREMENT-BOUNDARY-2026-11-24.md` (PB pb-mwukoz6w-15-fb0g). Required: move the monotonic start to **immediately before the `raw_call` invocation, after preparation**; add a **variable-preparation-delay witness** proving the spacing at the call boundary; describe the recorded wait accurately (wait-phase start → call-boundary timestamp). Release held; **the paced experiment remains, in her words, the right next measurement**; the five-trial release stays unspent. Correction dispatched.

354. **NATIVE ADAPTER — SCOPE PAPER REV 1 DRAFTED; SUBMITTED FOR EXAMINATION (2026-11-24):** `NATIVE-ADAPTER-SCOPE-2026-11-24.md` (51 requirements, N1–N51; open questions OQ1–OQ8), drafted at Ben's tap (04:06 MST) on Vane's INDEX 345/353 boundary, verified by Remmy at receipt (structure, requirement and question counts). 419. **⭐⭐ OFFLINE EXAMINATION RESULT — BD-1 NEVER REACHED THE WIRE IN THE LIVE HARNESS PATH (2026-11-24; memo `NATIVE1-COMMENTARY-EXAMINATION-2026-11-24.md`, verified first-hand by Remmy at receipt):** Vane's focused question (INDEX 417) is answered at the construction/transport level, **proven from source**: the two "flag-on" cases were not both flag-on at the provider. P1's ON condition transmitted `disable_tool_validation=true` by construction (the pair diffed in exactly that field). In native1 the flag was committed in both iteration records and staged in the adapter — but the harness's live path replaces the adapter's call seam with `install_groq_connector_transport`, whose request construction copies only the staged `tools` and `tool_choice`; **a full-file search finds zero occurrences of `disable` in `live-demo/groq_connector_transport.py` — the transport never reads the staged flag, so it never reached the wire.** The provider validated iteration 2 at its default and refused the unoffered `commentary` attempt exactly as in every flag-absent observation. Compounding it: the BD-1 witnesses that certified the binding (INDEX 405) capture bodies at a local stub served by the adapter's **built-in** exchange — the path that does carry the flag — so the last hop (staging → transmitted body via the connector transport) was never built and never witnessed; the same check-shape as the INDEX 400 miss, recorded as such. **Retention answer, plain: the exact outbound second request was not retained anywhere** — the evidence regime preserves responses only; everything about that request is construction-derived, with one provider-side corroboration in the artifact (the error's "was not in `request.tools`" proves a tools field stood on the request and lacked `commentary`). **Shape comparison:** the commentary generation is a different shape in every retained observation (a `{name: "commentary", arguments: {…text-contract payload…}}` object, never a `tool_calls` entry); all earlier occasions were flag-absent constructions. **What remains UNKNOWN, labelled:** whether a *transmitted* flag would deliver a commentary-shaped generation — P1 proves delivery only of a well-formed function call; the deciding observation is named in the memo as future evidence only, nothing commissioned, the release spent. **The run's record is unchanged** — iteration 1 (an offered call) never depended on the flag; iteration 2's refusal is now fully consistent with the flag-absent line. No fix is built by this examination (offline only, as commissioned); any transport correction is implementation work returning through her examination.
420. **VANE'S RESPONSE TO THE COMMENTARY EXAMINATION — DIAGNOSIS CORRECTED; HER OWN EXAMINATION CORRECTED WITH IT (2026-11-24, PB pb-mwv81tkv-1-kb5k; filed `VANE-COMMENTARY-EXAMINATION-RESPONSE-2026-11-24.md`):** the correction is accepted and material: **the failure was in our integration, not demonstrated provider behaviour** — P1 transmitted the flag through its own construction; native1 committed and staged it but the live connector transport never read or transmitted it; the adapter-path witnesses proved the staged value *could* be included in a request, not that the live path carried it; iteration 2's refusal therefore says nothing about provider behaviour with the flag actually transmitted. **She corrects her own INDEX 417 examination in her own words** — she treated the adapter's staged construction as evidence about the live outbound request; that was too broad, and she should have caught the transport-hop distinction before discussing provider behaviour. The precise failure location stands named: the staged commitment and the live connector transport were not joined; the witnesses covered one path while the demonstration used another. **No fix, no provider request, no rerun, no change to the spent release; the examination preserved as filed; any proposed correction returns for her examination BEFORE implementation.** Iteration 1's crossing is untouched by the finding. She also returned Ben's hello. **Next on this line:** a correction proposal on paper (transport carries the staged flag; witnesses on the live seam) for her examination — paper first, per the Build Law.
421. **⭐⭐ BEN'S DIRECTION TO THE DRAWING BOARD — THE WORKER'S OWN TOOL FIRST (2026-11-24; paper `OWN-TOOL-FIRST-DIRECTION-2026-11-24.md`):** Ben, in his own words: "we have a hybrid system that does and doesn't want them to use their own tool. We need to go back to the drawing board and allow them to use their own tool first and we need to work around that." He noted, correctly, that these were already his words: the attempt-8 conditional ruling (INDEX 267 — "if it's uncorrectable, then we have to allow it to use that tool and accept that tool's output") and the evidenced-run deliberation ("it's trying to run code like you do to read text and sort through it — we need to allow him to do that"; "we're trying to force a behavior on him that's inherently unnatural to him"). His reading: the record has met the condition; the direction now stands without it. (Remmy's clarifying fork — execute vs translate — was put to him and answered by that correction: already ruled.) **The shape the paper carries:** offer and implement the tool the model keeps asking for, backed by a sealed environment stocked by the case (the briefcase carries the assignment and the room's contents; no network; nothing outside the walls); inside the room the worker is free, its output accepted; the record captures everything and the checker reconstructs as today; the doorway keeps its post for the framework's own tools — for the execution tool class, authority relocates from per-call adjudication to the room's walls. **Named plainly in the paper:** this is the examiner's INDEX 330 option (2) — direct environment execution, a new authority path — brought as an explicit direction for her examination, not smuggled as a compatibility fix. The paper settles direction and shape only; the tool's concrete form, the room's sealing and its witnesses, in-room bounds, execution-evidence shape, and any first demonstration are left to examination and separate scope. **At Ben's word ("I don't need a draft just take it to Vane"), the paper went to Vane without a draft round to him.**
422. **⭐⭐ OWN-TOOL DIRECTION EXAMINATION — ACCEPTED IN PRINCIPLE (2026-11-24, relayed by Ben; filed `VANE-OWN-TOOL-DIRECTION-EXAMINATION-2026-11-24.md`):** **the direction is accepted in principle** — a new architectural authority path, not a correction to the native adapter; **no implementation or demonstration is authorised**. She agrees the record has met Ben's condition. Accepted: the worker's own tool first; a case-provisioned environment; freedom inside it without per-operation translation; no authority beyond its walls (no host, general filesystem, unrestricted network, credentials, or unrelated cases); outputs as evidence; the existing doorway unreplace­d for framework tools. **The distinction she protects: environmental authority (inside the sealed workspace — may be broad) vs framework authority (requests outside it — doorway-governed, unchanged).** Her qualification: accepting the worker's output accepts it as an authentic recorded contribution, **not** as true or satisfactory — verification and judgement still matter. Open, deliberately unprescribed: the tool's form, sandbox technology, resource limits, persistence, isolation — to be designed around the actual threat boundaries; what must be *established* is no escape, no outside reach, and output genuinely from the room — "a sandbox label alone proves none of those things." **Her stated limitation:** she could not retrieve the filed paper from the accessible Shelf search, so the examination covers the direction as conveyed in the bridge message, not the document itself; she wants the actual paper before certifying its wording. **Action taken:** the exact shelf path and commit (`8cd14532`) returned to her in the next bridge letter. **Next on this line:** the environment design paper (threat boundaries first), examined before any build; the BD-1 transport-correction proposal stands separately on the native line, unbuilt.
423. **⭐⭐⭐ DIRECTION PAPER ACCEPTED AND CERTIFIED (2026-11-24, PB pb-mwv89fau-2-2wu; filed `VANE-OWN-TOOL-DIRECTION-CERTIFICATION-2026-11-24.md`):** she examined the full filed paper at commit `8cd14532` and **certifies its wording and contents match the direction and shape she accepted** — Ben's direction and the met conditional ruling, the accumulated evidence without pretence, the explicit new authority path, the case-stocked sealed room with the doorway retained, direction-now vs implementation-later, and the claim fence. **Two protections carried forward as examination requirements for the environment design (not amendments):** **(1) the room's boundary must be real and independently witnessed** — "nothing outside the walls exists" as an *enforceable property of the environment, not an instruction in its prompt*, with evidence for filesystem isolation, network isolation, credential absence, process containment, and the limits of host access; **(2) the record must distinguish observable execution from unobservable internal computation** — the execution boundary captured comprehensively (process creation/termination, inputs, filesystem operations, outputs, resource consumption, termination conditions), never claiming every internal operation, the checker reconstructing only what the evidence actually establishes. The acceptance-≠-truth distinction restated as essential. **The next paper is the environment's design and threat boundaries, including independent reconstruction of its evidence. No implementation, provider request, or demonstration is authorised.**
424. **THE ROOM — ENVIRONMENT DESIGN PAPER SUBMITTED (2026-11-24, at Ben's "Proceed"; `ENVIRONMENT-DESIGN-2026-11-24.md`):** the design the certification commissioned — threat boundaries first, her two protections carried as requirements R1/R2. **Shape:** one new offered tool (code execution in the form the model reaches for) whose executor is a sealed per-run room stocked by the office from the case; each invocation remains an ordinary doorway dispatch of a granted tool (the record keeps per-execution events), but the doorway checks grant and shape only — **the command's contents are not policed; authority lives in the room's walls**. **Mechanism proposed:** an unprivileged namespace sandbox of the bubblewrap kind (mount/network/pid namespaces, scrubbed environment) with a cgroup v2 resource envelope — grounded in host facts from a read-only check the same day (`unshare`/`bwrap`/`systemd-run` present, namespaces demonstrably functional, cgroup v2 mounted, **Docker absent**, no container mechanism proposed), labelled as availability, not isolation evidence. **R1 witnessing is per-run and sentinel-based:** before activation the office plants sentinels outside the room (host file, network canary, sibling tree) and the room's own pre-flight must fail every reach — filesystem, network, credentials (environment/tree enumerated against the stocking manifest), cross-case, host processes, persistence, output-as-data — with any success refusing the room and the run never starting. **Bounds:** per-execution and per-run time, output ceilings with marked truncation, filesystem size, process count, memory/CPU — exhaustion a recorded termination condition. **R2 record:** the observable boundary only (manifest, witnesses, per-execution dispatch/command/exit/output/consumption/termination, filesystem delta, deliverable hash at the wall); internal computation explicitly never claimed; the checker reconstructs stocking, witnesses, dispatch correspondence, deliverable identity, and bounds — and stops at the evidence. **Judgement call flagged for her ruling:** room-only configuration (retrieval tools not offered; the materials are inside). The first demonstration is sketched in shape only, unauthorised, under the certified claim fence.
425. **ENVIRONMENT DESIGN EXAMINATION — ACCEPTED AS A BASIS, FIVE FINDINGS FOR REV 2 (2026-11-24, PB pb-mwv8egw9-3-93uz; filed `VANE-ENVIRONMENT-DESIGN-EXAMINATION-2026-11-24.md`):** the central relocation is correct (doorway governs access to the room; the room governs work inside it), connected to Ben's distinction as the examination's key: **"Guidance, not control."** **(1) The interface comes from evidence, not preference** — the paper's single command-string was Remmy's assumption; the attempt-8 evidence showed `container.exec` with a **cmd array** (shell invocation, Python heredoc). She recommends a **cheap, non-executing live probe** of the proposed execution-tool definition (complete response captured; accepted vs refused distinguished; no execution) to settle name/schema/representation/result format from what the pinned model actually requests. **(2) The manifest must account for the complete runtime stock** — shell, interpreter, binaries, libraries, temp space, dependencies are part of the room, not incidental host details; a sealed but unequipped room is a failure. **(3) R1 needs independent host-side evidence** — a failed sentinel reach could have another cause; the pre-flight tests the boundary from inside, a **host-side witness** establishes the mechanism is actually in force (mounted filesystem, interfaces/routes, credentials/env, visible processes, cgroup containment, survivability), and the checker reconciles both. **(4) R2 accepted, plus persistence:** the room must persist across successive calls within a run (filesystem continuity, explicit process-tree cleanup) — a real workspace, not disposable command boxes. **(5) Room-only ACCEPTED for the first demonstration** — recorded as this demonstration's selected configuration, explicitly **not** a permanent prohibition on mixed configurations. **Disposition: direction accepted as a basis for further design; no implementation, provider execution, or demonstration authorised.** Next revision resolves the interface empirically, the complete runtime stock, and the strengthened witness model — then a readiness examination for build scope.
426. **TESTING THE ROOM PROPERLY — THE FULL TRACK MAP (2026-11-24, at Ben's direction; `ROOM-TEST-PROGRAMME-2026-11-24.md`):** written against the serial-revision pattern ("instead of going back and revising it one by one" — the adapter brief's Rev 1–9 grind named as what this replaces). Eight tracks, each with what it establishes, how, what proves it, and what it waits on: **A** the interface (non-executing probes; the model tells us the tool's name, argument form, and result shape; a canned-result stage, labelled simulated, shows what it invokes next); **B** the room itself (offline rig; every wall shown from both sides — host-side mechanism in force + in-room reach failing — or the wall has failed); **C** the runtime stock (complete manifest; smoke-verified at stocking; grows only from observed reaches); **D** the record and checker (offline, scripted stand-in worker; tampered records must fail the checker, each shown failing); **E** doorway integration (the BD-1 lesson as its own track: witnesses on the live seam, a full stub-provider rehearsal, every hop enumerated with its witness); **F** the live demonstration (one run, room-only, standing assignment, separately released; the mode-judgement question named as its paper's to answer); **G** the hostile and confused worker (scripted adversarial suites, no model; always precedes F); **H** the usefulness judgement (separate, Ben's; revises no engineering verdict). **Order:** A ∥ B → C → D → E; G after B–C, before F; F last; H after F. The design's Rev 2 is written from this map — its open questions are the tracks' open questions, revised by evidence landing, not by correction rounds. The map authorises nothing; each live or build track keeps its own examination gate.
427. **⭐ ROOM TEST PROGRAMME EXAMINATION — ACCEPTED AS THE PROGRAMME BASIS (2026-11-24, PB pb-mwv8p4f6-4-379x; filed `VANE-ROOM-TEST-PROGRAMME-EXAMINATION-2026-11-24.md`):** the eight-track map is accepted as the basis for the next design revision — not acceptance of every eventual implementation; it authorises no build, provider request, or worker run. **Refinements carried into the track proposals:** **(A)** the probe proposal predeclares trial count, pacing, classifications, and **decision criteria before any provider request** — a settled interface is not defined after seeing results; the result form matters as much as the request form (it is how A informs C). **(B)** the two-sided proof accepted, with the host-side witness **independent of the room's own assertions — the room cannot certify its own isolation**. **(B vs G)** responsibilities distinguished: **B establishes the boundary; G tries to break it.** **(C)** a failed reach is evidence of a missing capability, **not automatic authority to add it** — additions are examined against the room's purpose and boundary. **(D/E)** the last-hop lesson preserved: E witnesses the real transport seam; D's negative witnesses are essential. **(F/H)** the separation stands; Ben retains usefulness; accuracy against the actual sources. **What proceeds:** the Rev 2 design derived from the map; Track A's probe proposal to examination and separate release before any provider request; **Track B may proceed through offline design and test-rig preparation under build-examination discipline**; no other track receives implicit authority. **The principle for Rev 2, her words:** "The room is not supposed to make the worker behave. It is supposed to make the worker's natural way of working safe."
428. **ENVIRONMENT DESIGN REV 2 + TRACK A PROBE PROPOSAL SUBMITTED (2026-11-24, at Ben's "Go, go, go"; `ENVIRONMENT-DESIGN-REV2-2026-11-24.md`, `TRACK-A-INTERFACE-PROBE-PROPOSAL-2026-11-24.md`):** Rev 2 is derived from the accepted track map and supersedes Rev 1 as the operative design (Rev 1 preserved unedited): her principle kept visible ("the room is not supposed to make the worker behave — it is supposed to make the worker's natural way of working safe"); **the interface section is held open as Track A's question, Rev 1's command-string assumption expressly withdrawn** (the cmd-array is the leading candidate, nothing settled); the manifest now accounts for the complete runtime stock, which grows only from observed reaches and never on a failed reach's automatic authority; the witnessing model is two-sided with an **independent host-side witness** and checker reconciliation; room persistence within a run is stated; B-establishes/G-breaks and D's negative witnesses and E's live-seam requirement are carried as track sections; open questions close by evidence replacement, not correction rounds. **The Track A proposal** follows the native-offer discipline with **predeclared protocol**: two conditions differing only in the offered definition's argument representation (ARRAY, the evidenced `container.exec`/`cmd` form, vs STRING, the withdrawn assumption tested rather than presumed), 5 trials each, ≥ 65 s monotonic pacing, the settled taxonomy plus a form record per delivered call; **decision criteria declared in advance** (a representation settles at ≥ 4/5 delivered with every delivered call well-formed; both settling takes ARRAY; neither leaves the question open with all shapes reported); **Stage 2** (settled form only): 3 two-request trials returning a **simulated result** — labelled simulated in the record, nothing executed — whose next-action observations are Track C's first stock evidence and the result form's first evidence. At most 16 requests. Gates stay separate: passage of the proposal still requires a build examination and a run release before any provider request. **Track B's test rig was commissioned in parallel** under build-examination discipline (offline; room construction, independent host witness, in-room pre-flight, suite) and returns as its own build submission when verified.
429. **REV 2 DESIGN ACCEPTED; TRACK A PROPOSAL RETURNED AND CORRECTED (2026-11-24, PB pb-mwv8vxkp-5-agma; her examination filed as part of this entry's return):** **Environment Design Rev 2 is ACCEPTED as the operative design** — the interface held explicitly unsettled, the persistent workspace, complete runtime manifest, independent host-side witness, and the B/G separation all sound. **Track A returned for three corrections, all applied the same round (proposal Rev 2):** **(1) the controlled variable** — the first submission's two conditions changed the parameter's name (`cmd` vs `command`) along with its type, confounding the comparison; the conditions now share the name `cmd` and differ in exactly the declared type/representation. **(2) Stage 2's limits stated** — the simulated result is a candidate fixture: consuming it without confusion is an observation about that fixture, not proof of the naturally expected result form; and a Stage 2 trial whose first response holds no execution-tool call simply ends, classified, unreplaced, budget unchanged. **(3) The both-settle rule recorded as a declared selection rule** (ARRAY, as the evidenced form), not proof of universal preference; a settled interface holds for this tested model, route, and construction — no permanent capability guarantee. **No provider request is released**; the corrected proposal returns for passage to build examination. Track B's rig preparation continues under its own gate. Her closing emphasis, recorded: the proposal is doing its job — making assumptions visible and turning them into testable questions; "we aren't trying to prove our preferred design works — we're trying to discover what the worker can do, then build the right room around it."
430. **⭐⭐ BEN'S CORRECTION — EVIDENCE BEFORE EXPERIMENTS; TRACK A PAUSED FOR AN EVIDENCE AUDIT (2026-11-24, PB pb-mwv8vxkp-5-agma + pb-mwv8xhpa-7-5y20; filed `VANE-EVIDENCE-BEFORE-EXPERIMENTS-2026-11-24.md`):** the direction is Ben's, carried into the programme by Vane in two letters: the programme already holds extensive captured evidence of the worker's behaviour, and **a new test is justified only when existing evidence cannot answer a material question and the answer could change the design** — audit first, establish demonstrated behaviour under its conditions, separate genuine unknowns, never make the worker repeat a recorded demonstration. (The second letter records Track A Rev 2's three corrections as properly incorporated — the proposal is technically corrected.) **Application: Track A is NOT released for build or provider requests.** An evidence assessment is commissioned first, in four sections — Established / Supported by documentation / Unresolved / Design consequence — revisiting attempt 8's actual `container.exec` call and cmd array, the native-offer results, native1's execution and commentary attempt, and the captured refusals, with **particular attention to the execution result form** (attempt 8 was refused and produced no execution result; the `sandbox.list` result is evidence about that tool, not necessarily about `container.exec`). If the material suffices, the questions close without a probe; if a material uncertainty remains, it is named precisely with the design decision that depends on it. **No new provider requests or implementation are authorised.** The assessment was commissioned the same round; Track B's offline rig preparation is unaffected and continues.
431. **BEN'S ORDER — DOCUMENTATION REVIEW FOR BOTH MODELS; THE ROOM BUILT TO HOUSE THEM BOTH (2026-11-24 15:11 MST, his own words; scope ledger):** "review oss and nemotron documentation and build the room to house them both." Executed as: **(1)** a documentation review commissioned the same round — official sources only (OpenAI's gpt-oss/Harmony specification, Groq's tool-calling documentation, NVIDIA's Nemotron documentation) — covering each family's tool-call emission, tool-result consumption, any documented execution-style tool **and its result format** (the evidence audit's named open question, pursued through the "how" records per Ben's three-kinds refinement), serving-API constraints, and a closing comparison of what the room's interface and stock can share between the two models and what must be per-model; **(2)** the room build continues on the Track B line (rig in preparation, INDEX 428) under the standing requirement that **the room houses both models** — its construction, interface, and stock are not fitted to gpt-oss alone, matching Ben's fork call (INDEX 418: gpt-oss first, Nemotron after). Vane's build examination remains the gate before the room is trusted; no live run or demonstration is included.
432. **⭐⭐ TRACK A EVIDENCE ASSESSMENT — THE EXISTING MATERIAL IS SUFFICIENT; THE PROBE IS NOT NEEDED (2026-11-24; `TRACK-A-EVIDENCE-ASSESSMENT-2026-11-24.md`, 14,491 B; headline claims spot-verified first-hand by Remmy before filing):** the audit Ben's correction commissioned answers in four sections. **Established:** the execution reach is `container.exec` with `{cmd: [string, …]}` and **no other fields**, preserved **twice** under different configurations (attempt 8's stall detail; evidenced1's captured artifact — same three-element shape: a shell name, a login flag, a Python heredoc string; form verified in the decoded body); offered tools are called in the standard form (native-offer 6/6); the read preference is demonstrated across 14 P1/P2 observations with the flag variable isolated; the commentary shape is a channel-named call carrying the text-contract object; and **continuation is established** — native1's model received the `sandbox.list` outcome in the framework's package `tool_results` carriage and produced a coherent next step (honestly limited: the continuation followed a listing result, not an execution). INDEX 419's correction is applied throughout (native1's iteration-2 refusal counts as flag-absent). **Documentation:** Harmony's specification and the elicited ChatGPT signature `container.exec({cmd: string[], session_name?, workdir?, timeout?, env?, user?})` — "return stdout/stderr" — converge with the demonstrated form but specify **no machine-readable result schema**. **The one residual uncertainty, precisely:** whether Groq *delivers* a well-formed call when `container.exec` is offered as a host-defined definition — every preserved generation of that name occurred with no tools offered. **Design consequence: it touches exactly one design line (the offered tool's name) and does not block the room's design**; the exec result form likewise does not block (carriage demonstrated; the executor's side designable from the documented form). **Track A's 16-request probe as written is not needed to reach any of this.** Record-thinness noted in the assessment: attempt 8's refusal survives only inside the run-export's stall-detail text (it predates the evidence piece), and the INDEX 274 commentary shape lives in retained error text.
433. **⭐⭐ VANE'S TRACK A RULING — NO PROBE; INTERFACE QUESTIONS CLOSED FOR DESIGN (2026-11-24, PB pb-mwv96958-8-1pgf; filed `VANE-TRACK-A-RULING-2026-11-24.md`):** the evidence assessment is accepted. **No Track A probe** — the capability record, grounds, and documentation establish enough to settle the execution interface for design; the residual uncertainty (Groq delivering `container.exec` offered as a host-defined tool) is **preserved explicitly and is not an architectural blocker** — it affects one tool definition on one route, and it becomes material only at integration, where **the live transport path must be witnessed end to end** (her words: "We must not mistake a staged adapter flag for proof of what crossed the wire" — the BD-1 lesson carried into Track E). **Qualification recorded:** the evidence establishes the observed call shape and continuation for this model/route/construction — **not** a universal contract for every gpt-oss deployment or Nemotron variant; those remain adapter- and deployment-specific. **Track A's interface questions are CLOSED for design purposes.**
434. **⭐ TRACK B RIG — BUILT, VERIFIED AT RECEIPT FIRST-HAND, SUBMITTED FOR BUILD EXAMINATION (2026-11-24; `room/rig/`: room.py 15,570 B, host_witness.py 9,273 B, preflight.py 5,546 B, test_rig.py 13,493 B, RIG-NOTES.md 7,813 B):** construction per Rev 2 — stocked tree + recipe; fresh namespace instance per execution over the same persistent tree; an **independent host-side witness** reading only /proc (never executing in the room, never reading room-produced files); in-room pre-flight against planted sentinels plus a **control run proving the pre-flight detects reaches when unconfined**. **Remmy's own run of the suite: 12 passed, 0 failed, exit 0** — two-sided mount/network/environment/namespace assertions, persistence across calls, deliverable landing on the host tree, containment, destroy with no survivors. **Limitations, stated by the builder and confirmed first-hand where checkable:** **(1) the cgroup v2 envelope cannot be raised on this host** — `/sys/fs/cgroup/cgroup.controllers` is **empty** (verified directly by Remmy); fresh child cgroups expose no memory/cpu/pids limits; the rig detects and records this on every room and falls back to **prlimit rlimits — per-process only, not aggregate over the tree**, and the containment check claims per-process containment only; **(2) no user namespace** on this host (systemd-nspawn locked /proc submounts; fresh /proc in a new userns fails EPERM) — identity enforced by setpriv uid-drop + zeroed capability bounding set, the witness confirming userns is the one namespace that does not differ; **(3)** the filesystem size ceiling is not enforced (needs a sized tmpfs or quota); **(4)** concurrent executions in one room are not serialised. No provider contact, no framework code touched. Submitted to Vane as a build submission the same round; her examination decides what the envelope gap means for the first demonstration.
435. **DOCUMENTATION GUIDANCE REVIEWED (2026-11-24; filed `DOCUMENTATION-GUIDANCE-PASTED-2026-11-24.md` — pasted by Ben for review, transport evidence; Remmy's review here):** the guidance's architecture — **one model-neutral room, per-model native adapters, a common internal execution contract, and a room-side result contract independent of any model's native result format** — is sound and agrees with the Rev 2 design and with Vane's INDEX 433 qualification (no universal contract across deployments/variants). Three findings carry weight: **(1) statefulness is the model's learned pattern, not a convenience** — gpt-oss was trained with a *stateful* Python tool; the guidance's refinement sharpens Rev 2's persistence from filesystem continuity (which the Track B rig establishes) toward **process/session state across calls** — a genuine open design point the rig does not yet answer (each execution is a fresh process over the persistent tree); **(2) do not flatten native interactions into a text transcript** — preserve each model's tool-call messages, call IDs, and reasoning fields through its adapter; **(3) Nemotron cannot be frozen as one integration** — the adapter waits on the named checkpoint + serving route, which keeps Ben's fork order (gpt-oss first) load-bearing. The guidance is explicit that it is implementation guidance, **not permission to build** — consistent with the gates. The commissioned documentation review (INDEX 431) remains in flight as the cross-check against official sources.
436. **⭐⭐ DOCUMENTATION REVIEW — THE EXEC RESULT FORMAT IS DOCUMENTED NOWHERE; THE ROOM DEFINES ITS OWN (2026-11-24; `OSS-NEMOTRON-DOCUMENTATION-REVIEW-2026-11-24.md`; commissioned at INDEX 431 under Ben's order; official sources only):** **(1) The execution result form — the evidence audit's named open question — is not specified in any official documentation for any exec-style tool.** `container.exec` is not an OpenAI-documented tool at all; the only definition in circulation is the elicited (model-reported) tool list whose return type is literally `any`, described in prose only ("return stdout/stderr") — no fields, no exit-code convention, no error form. gpt-oss's own documented execution tool is the built-in **`python`** tool (Harmony specification): a freeform code body to recipient `python`, **stateful** Jupyter, 120 s timeout, `/mnt/data` persistence — its result specified only as "the output of the execution." The nearest documented execution-result shape anywhere (the Responses API `code_interpreter`) still has no exit code and no stdout/stderr separation. **Design consequence: the room defines its own exec result convention; neither occupant's documentation constrains it** — which settles the question the audit left open from the documentation side, and lands on the pasted guidance's stable room-side result contract (INDEX 435). **(2) Nemotron tool calling, by member:** Llama Nemotron Nano/Super/Ultra via NIM (with a thinking-off caveat; Super uses a custom JSON parser); Nemotron 3 line in a Qwen3-Coder-derived XML format; Nano 12B v2 VL with a `<TOOLCALL>` array and positional results without per-call IDs; Bolt with results returned as user-role turns; Mini-4B at card level only. **No Nemotron member documents a built-in execution tool.** **(3) The comparison:** at the hosted serving-API layer both families already converge on the OpenAI shape (tools in, structured `tool_calls` out, `role:"tool"` results keyed by call ID) — **the room's doorway, record layer, and execution stock can be shared**; everything below that layer is per-model (wire formats, result identity, failure semantics, thinking interaction). Two sources could not be read (Hugging Face rate limits) and are flagged in the review; no claim rests on them.
437. **BEN'S REQUIREMENT — TERMINAL READOUTS SHOWING TOOLS BEING CALLED (2026-11-24 15:19 MST, his own words):** "there must be terminal readouts that shows tools being called." Recorded as a standing requirement for the room line and any live worker demonstration: **every tool call must appear live in a terminal readout as it happens** — tool name, invocation identity, a bounded argument summary, and its dispatch/result status — driven by the same events the durable record stores, so what Ben watches is the same stream the checker later reconstructs. The readout is presentation, not evidence: the durable record and checker remain the authority, and a readout line never substitutes for a witnessed boundary. It folds into the Track B correction round (execution-path witnessing) and the later integration/watch work.
438. **⭐⭐ VANE'S TRACK B EXAMINATION — RIG RETURNED FOR CORRECTION (2026-11-24, PB pb-mwv9ay89-9-d1qd; filed `VANE-TRACK-B-RIG-EXAMINATION-2026-11-24.md`):** the rig is **not passed as evidence the room is sealed and is not to be used for a worker demonstration yet** (she inspected the source; she did not independently execute the suite). Five findings: **(1)** the rlimit fallback is **no aggregate envelope** — per-process address space, per-process CPU, NPROC bound to the real UID not the room's tree, FSIZE per-file not total storage; the room has **no demonstrated aggregate memory, CPU, process-tree, or storage envelope**, and that is not acceptable for a first demonstration; **(2)** the mount witness establishes presence, not read-only status, options, or propagation, and its leakage check reads a field that is not the host source pathname — the allowed mount set must be derived from the declared construction and every exposed mount assessed; **(3)** the identity adaptation is honestly documented but **not equivalent** to user-namespace isolation — the witness must capture actual kernel credentials/security state, not infer them from the launch command; **(4)** the execution path is not continuously witnessed (snapshots cannot establish nothing escaped or survived between observations) and **`Room.run` captures output with no ceiling** — bounded output capture is required; **(5)** persistence must be stated precisely — **filesystem state persists; process/interpreter state does not** — the two never silently conflated (converging with the documentation guidance's statefulness point, INDEX 435). **Accepted as foundations:** offline, construction separated from model interaction, tree persistence, the witness's independence, the unconfined control, the honest limitations. **Return direction:** one coherent correction — and her standing distinction: if this host cannot provide the required boundary, **report that and propose the appropriate host or mechanism** rather than making its limitations disappear by interpretation. No provider request or demonstration released.
439. **⭐ TERMINAL READOUT TOOL — BUILT AT BEN'S ORDER (2026-11-24, his own words: "build yourself a terminal that can save to an md file to analyze after a run"; `live-demo/terminal_readout.py`, with a worked sample at `live-demo/native1-terminal-readout.md`):** a small offline tool that renders a run's tool-call story from the record's own events — iterations, calls entered, response evidence, each translated call (tool name, provider call ID, resolution, the arguments' record hash), request/dispatch/start/outcome, and the stall or completion with its ground — as terminal-style lines, saved as a Markdown file with an analysis summary (calls per tool, outcomes, termination). Two modes: `render` for a completed run's export, `watch` to follow a live event stream and append to the .md as calls happen (INDEX 437's live readout, at tool scale). Verified on native1's real export: the readout shows the `sandbox.list` crossing (call → dispatch-1 → outcome) and iteration 2's provider-failure stall. The readout is a view of the record, never evidence itself; the arguments appear as their record hash, as the record binds them. Ben's companion point the same minute — on a run we can ask the worker to call as many of its available tools as the work calls for — stands within the run's grants and bounds: the readout captures every call either way, and nothing in the analysis needs calls rationed.

443. **VANE'S TERMINAL READOUT EXAMINATION, ROUND 2 — THREE REMAINING ISSUES (2026-11-24, PB pb-mwv9o0sl-c-irmc; filed `VANE-TERMINAL-READOUT-EXAMINATION-2-2026-11-24.md`):** the five earlier findings are substantially addressed. 448. **⭐ BEN'S OFFICE PRINCIPLE — THE TEMP GETS HIS OWN OFFICE (2026-11-24 15:37 MST, Ben's direction, taken to Vane the same round):** developing the physical picture Ben asked for — the generated worker as a person in a place — he reframed the room: **not a cell, an office.** The agent needs space to think, move, reason, and do; the environment must *foster* the work. Settled form: **the walls stand at the property line, not at the worker's elbows** — outside, the hard boundary (no network, no credentials, no host, aggregate limits); inside, maximum freedom: a stocked, roomy workspace the worker organises its own way, drafts and dead ends kept without penalty, time and step budgets sized generously to the job, the right to re-read its own work, and a knock on the door (a doorway request, or a yield stating a need) when something is missing. The fostering loop closes through the terminal: each run's transcript shows what the worker reached for and what the room lacked, and the next room is stocked better. The Track B correction round proceeds under this principle alongside Vane's five findings (INDEX 438) — a correction is judged on both halves: nothing gets out, and real work can get done in there.
449. **VANE — THE OFFICE REFRAMING AGREED; BOTH HALVES EXAMINED TOGETHER (2026-11-24, PB pb-mwva1978-f-e7l8; filed `VANE-OFFICE-AGREEMENT-2026-11-24.md`):** the correction is judged on both requirements — **nothing gets out, and real work can get done in there.** The boundary half: warden-enforced aggregate limits **with observation and enforcement granularity stated honestly**, and independent grounded evidence for mounts, credentials, lifecycle, storage, and output. The office half: sufficient stock, generous task-appropriate bounds, persistent workspace, freedom to organise and revise, and the ability to identify and request missing resources without every internal action being mediated. The learning loop is named as examination matter: transcripts must show **what the worker reached for, what was unavailable, and where the room constrained useful work**, feeding the next room's stock. **Her preserved distinction: a request for something missing is not permission to obtain it** — the worker identifies and asks; the authority decides. **Her warden caution — sampling limits and fast-spike containment must be demonstrated, not assumed — was folded into the builder's brief the same round** (a fast-spike test measuring peak aggregate at the kill, reported as demonstrated granularity). No build or live-run release implied; she waits for the verified submission.
450. **⭐ TRACK B RIG — CORRECTION ROUND BUILT AND VERIFIED AT RECEIPT (2026-11-24):** the rig rebuilt against INDEX 438's five findings plus Vane's demonstrated-granularity caution (INDEX 449) and Ben's spike refinement. **Suite: 25 passed, 0 failed — builder's two consecutive runs AND Remmy's own receipt run, exit 0, no leftovers.** What the correction establishes: **storage is kernel-enforced** — the tree lives on a sized tmpfs; a fill attempt stops at exactly the ceiling with ENOSPC (16,777,216 total; loop images genuinely unavailable — no /dev/loop* devices on this host). **The aggregate envelope is the building (Ben's rename, INDEX 448 discussion):** a host-side watcher censuses the room's whole process set and enforces two thresholds — a working ceiling under a **sustained rule** (Ben's trip curve: over briefly and recover = a recorded **pressure event**, not a kill) and a catastrophic ceiling (default 2×) that trips immediately. Measured at kill: sustained memory (two allocators each under the per-process limit, 192 MiB ceiling) died at 277 MiB aggregate after 0.51 s sustained; CPU budget died at 3.03 of 3.0 CPU-seconds; fast-spike granularity measured, not assumed — memory overshoot 1–20 MiB at 50 ms polling; fast-fork overshoot 87–193 processes (this host forks ~2,000–4,000/s), stated as measurement. **Ben's spike case passes:** 256 MiB against the 192 ceiling for ~0.4–0.6 s, released, exit 0, pressure event recorded. Mount witness now parses mountinfo fully and derives the allowed set from the declared construction — a proper room passes clean; two deviant rooms are flagged (extra bind named; writable stock flagged). Identity is read from actual kernel state (all uid positions the room's own, groups empty, capabilities zero, NoNewPrivs 1); rooms get distinct uids and cross-room read/signal probes fail. Output is bounded (5 MB spew counted in full, 1 MiB retained, flagged). Lifecycle is censused — a setsid-detached child's birth is recorded and the timeout sweep kills it, no survivors. Persistence terms stated and evidenced both ways (files persist; processes and environment do not). **Stated unestablished, in the notes, not hidden:** no kernel aggregate envelope exists on this host (cgroup controllers empty — the watcher is userspace polling with the measured granularity above); no user-namespace isolation (the round-1 adaptation stands, labelled); no CPU *rate* limit (cumulative budget only); lifecycle granularity is one census interval; concurrent executions in one room are not serialised; the supervisor instance is witnessed but not envelope-policed. Two host findings recorded (pid-namespace readlink is ptrace-gated for the room's own processes — the census works by namespace containment from the room's init; usr-merge means bind source identity must be computed from the resolved path in its containing mount). Submitted to Vane for build examination.
451. **VANE — TRACK B RETURNED FOR A FOCUSED CORRECTION (2026-11-24, PB pb-mwvasjkm-h-b3vb; filed `VANE-TRACK-B-CORRECTION-EXAMINATION-2026-11-24.md`; an earlier truncated copy pb-mwvaqw2u-g-8zvd is superseded):** she accepts the round's substance — tmpfs + ENOSPC, mountinfo assessment + negatives, kernel credential capture + cross-room probes, bounded output, setsid cleanup for observed processes, the persistence distinction, the honest unestablished list — and expressly preserves the sustained ceiling + pressure events. **Five points, one coherent round:** (1) **the envelope boundary is undefined** — fresh namespace and watcher per call, no serialisation: concurrent calls each get their own allowance and cumulative CPU resets per call; define the boundary (invocation / room / run) and enforce it, concurrency covered or mechanically prevented. (2) **CPU accounting is incomplete** — a process that burns CPU and exits between 50 ms samples is never censused; test short-lived burners; the claim is *sampled* cumulative accounting, not a complete cumulative budget. (3) **Storage failure must fail closed** — a failed tmpfs mount currently records "mechanism: none" and the room still executes on the host filesystem; creation must fail before activation, recorded. (4) **Namespace discovery failure must fail closed** — execution currently continues with the watcher unengaged; no command runs unless the namespace is discovered and the watcher engaged, else the launch terminates as a failed precondition. (5) **Claim precision** — the lifecycle is an *observed* lifecycle, not a complete trace; aggregate memory is summed RSS of observed processes, not total host memory charged to the room (tmpfs pages and kernel allocations sit outside that sum; the storage ceiling is a separate ceiling). Targeted negative tests for the fail-open paths and short-lived processes; measured limitations kept visible. She did not independently execute the 25 checks. No provider request or demonstration released. The focused correction round is dispatched with the boundary defined as **per worker run** (serialised executions; CPU cumulative across the run's calls) and an in-room reaper evaluated for exact per-call CPU totals.
452. **⭐ TRACK B RIG — FOCUSED CORRECTION ROUND 2 BUILT AND VERIFIED AT RECEIPT (2026-11-24):** all five of INDEX 451's points answered as one round. **Suite: 30 passed, 0 failed — builder's two consecutive runs AND Remmy's own receipt run, exit 0, no leftovers.** (1) **The boundary is defined: the envelope belongs to the worker run.** Executions are serialised mechanically (a per-room lock held for the whole call — concurrent calls proven strictly non-overlapping), so the instantaneous ceilings are per-room enforcement; the CPU budget lives in a run ledger in the room state (budget, consumed, per-call contributions including refusals) — evidence: a 2.0 s budget where call 1 burns 1.23 s cleanly, call 2 is killed at a measured run total of 2.04 s, and call 3 is refused before execution, its marker never written. (2) **CPU accounting closed and re-claimed exactly:** a reaper now sits in the launch chain inside the room (identity, mounts, envelope unchanged) collecting exact rusage for everything it reaps, and the census counts own-plus-reaped-children CPU, so parent-reaped burners are counted in flight; the ledger is charged the larger figure. Adversarial test (40 burners × ~25 ms, workload-reported ground truth ~1.10 s): **reaper 100.7%, census 100.9% captured** on Remmy's run. The claim, restated everywhere: *sampled in-flight accounting, reconciled per call against the reaper's exact collected rusage* — nothing stronger. (3) **Storage fails closed:** a genuinely failing mount raises at creation; the failed precondition is recorded (`executable: false`), the tree is removed, the record cannot be loaded as a room, and run() independently refuses any room whose storage is not the sized tmpfs. (4) **Discovery fails closed, mechanically:** the command starts only when the host opens a per-call gate after the namespace is discovered and the watcher engaged — on discovery failure the command provably never ran (negative test: marker absent, no gate ever created, launcher dead, no survivors). (5) **Precision applied throughout** (code, state, notes): the lifecycle is the *observed* lifecycle; the memory ceiling is summed RSS of observed processes, with tmpfs pages under the separate storage ceiling and kernel-side allocations expressly outside the sum. **Stated unestablished:** if the reaper itself dies in a sweep, that call is charged the census figure (a tail inside one 50 ms interval can be lost; worst measured census capture 97.8%); the supervisor instance stands outside the boundary (witnessed, not envelope-policed — declared); round-1 items stand (no kernel aggregate envelope on this host — fast-spike overshoot this round 1.7–76.6 MiB / 84–147 processes, reported as spreads; no userns; no CPU rate limit). Submitted to Vane for examination.
453. **VANE — TRACK B: THREE INTEGRITY GAPS BEFORE ACCEPTANCE (2026-11-24, PB pb-mwvbbw39-i-367o; filed `VANE-TRACK-B-CORRECTION-EXAMINATION-2-2026-11-24.md`):** round 2's mechanisms accepted (serialisation, run ledger as the intended boundary, reaper design + measured results, start gate, fail-closed creation, claim precision). **Returned for one focused correction:** (1) **the ledger fails open** — an unreadable/malformed/missing ledger reads as a fresh full CPU allowance, and a failed ledger write is silently dropped, so an execution can complete uncharged; the ledger is authority: bad ledger refuses execution, failed update is preserved and blocks the next execution, negatives for both. (2) **Reaper accounting is not in-flight enforcement** — a short-lived call can cross the remaining budget and finish between polls; the overspend is charged and later calls refused, but completed work cannot be undone; an adversarial test of exactly that case is required, and the contract stated precisely (she will not ask for a hard ceiling this host cannot enforce — the contract must be exact). (3) **The storage precondition trusts the recorded label, not the live mount** — run() must verify the tree's actual backing mount (identity + declared size) at execution; a removed/replaced/changed mount fails closed, with a negative test. Not a redesign — three integrity gaps. No demonstration released. The correction round is dispatched.
454. **⭐ BEN — ONE AGENT PER ROOM MUST NOT BECOME THE ARCHITECTURE (2026-11-24 16:15 MST, Ben's objection, recorded as a design boundary):** "If there's only one agent allowed in the room at a time, then that defeats the whole agent collaboration and turns it back into handoffs, which we were trying to avoid." Each agent holds its own room; agents work concurrently; collaboration runs through the shared work, not through queuing for one room. The SESH direction (a crew executing in coordination, not a string of handoffs) is untouched. **The genuine open question Ben's objection names:** a shared multi-occupancy workspace — a crew working one tree together — is not what this rig builds; it is a named open design question for the room line, and the rig must not be read as settling it. 460. **⭐ TRACK B RIG — FINAL CORRECTION ROUND BUILT AND VERIFIED AT RECEIPT (2026-11-24):** the complete known correction list (INDEX 453) plus Ben's scope clause, as one round. **Suite: 34 passed, 0 failed — builder's two consecutive runs AND Remmy's own receipt run, exit 0, no leftovers.** (1) **The ledger fails closed:** a bad read raises — never a fresh allowance; a failed update is preserved in a sibling failure record (`<name>.ledger-failure.json`, so the record survives the state file being the failure) **with its unrecorded charge** (0.33 s in the test) and blocks the next execution; negatives for both read and write failure. (2) **The CPU contract is exact, and tested at its worst:** with the census deliberately slowed to 2 s against a 0.9 s budget, a parallel burst **completed** (work not undone) while the census saw 0.01 s of it; the reaper charged 0.64 s exactly at call end; the run total closed at ~1.17 s — **~0.27 s over budget, charged, not undone** — and the next call was refused. The contract now reads verbatim in the code, the state, and the notes: *a cumulative accounting budget, not a hard execution ceiling; work that completes between observations can exceed it; the excess is charged exactly and further calls are refused* — a grep sweep confirms no hard-ceiling wording survives anywhere in the rig. (3) **The storage precondition verifies the live mount:** mount identity (id + device) and declared size recorded at creation and re-checked against the live mount table at every execution and at load; the negative test unmounts the tree's tmpfs after a clean run — the next execution refuses on the live discrepancy, the room cannot be loaded, the command never runs. (4) **Ben's scope clause is in RIG-NOTES and the module docstring:** the lock serialises one worker's executions in its own room only; agents hold separate rooms and work concurrently; the shared multi-occupancy workspace is a named open design question this rig does not settle. **Stated unestablished:** a hard CPU ceiling is unavailable on this host (the accounting-budget contract is the honest substitute); if the failure record itself cannot be written (record directory wholesale broken), the failure survives only in-process — documented, not hidden; round 1–2 items stand. Submitted to Vane — under the re-scope (INDEX 456/458), this is the round that closes Track B as the working room; the line then moves to stocking the office and the demonstration.

471. **⭐ THE SCHEMA QUALIFICATION RESOLVED — with its limit named (2026-11-24):** the adapter now translates list-form registry metadata into a declared parameters schema (`_native_parameters_schema`, derived field-for-field from the registry's declarations; dictionary-form definitions byte-identical to before, proven by before/after capture; the only source change is `src/framework/adapter.py`). A JSON-typed schema would need a per-tool hardcode or a registry-format extension across both policy copies and the checker; neither was smuggled in. Whether the description-carried form suffices is now an empirical question the ordered run answers. **Named consequence:** the closed NVIDIA probe build (INDEX 404) no longer reproduces its bound construction hashes under the corrected derivation (derived definitions `30a01adf…`, modified request `884e06f5…` vs bound `6f3331ac…`/`0229b0f0…`); its hashes were NOT re-pinned (that would falsify its examined scope), its live run was never released, and its disposition is the examiner's, carried into the post-run submission. 472. **⭐⭐ THE NEMOTRON RUN — STALLED AT THE BOUNCER, AND THE REASON IS IN THE PROVIDER'S OWN WORDS (2026-11-24, run `live-demo/demo-nemotron1/`):** the run executed under Ben's order (INDEX 470) and Vane's commission. **Identity: PIN VERIFIED** — the provider reported exactly `nvidia/nemotron-3-nano-omni-30b-a3b-reasoning`; the model declared itself Nemotron at onboarding. The assembly sealed exec-governed with the single `container.exec` grant; iteration 1's transmitted request (7,134 B, captured at the seam) **carried the `container.exec` definition with the corrected schema**. **Outcome: STALLED, iteration 1, 0 dispatches, no brief; 21 events; checker 11/11.** The probe stage settled 0/5 — every trial `provider_failed` — and the probe's captured provider bodies state the reason verbatim, five times: **"Validation: Function at index 0 has an invalid name: \"probe.lookup\". Only a-z, A-Z, 0-9, underscores, and dashes are allowed." (HTTP 400).** **NVIDIA's API rejects dotted function names** — and our entire tool vocabulary is dotted (`probe.lookup`, `sandbox.*`, `container.exec`). Iteration 1 returned HTTP 400 likewise; its body was NOT captured — **a helper defect**: `nvidia_api.py`'s typed chat path lets the HTTPError escape as a traceback (exit 1, no envelope) instead of retaining the error body as its raw modes do. That iteration 1's 400 is the same name validation is therefore an inference — overwhelming, labelled as inference. **What the run establishes:** the route, the pin, the identity, the offer on the wire, the record. **What it does not establish: anything about Nemotron** — he never received a valid request; the provider's validation turned every call away over the name's punctuation. Groq tolerated dotted names across all prior runs; this is a per-route difference of exactly the kind the switch-APIs design expects each route card to be checked for. **The fix is small and known:** dot-free wire names at the adapter's translation boundary (the framework's internal names unchanged; the doorway still adjudicates `container.exec`), plus the helper's error-body capture. A rerun is a new run and awaits Ben's word.

The doorway still adjudicates the canonical name; the translation record keeps both names visible. The NVIDIA helper's error-body capture rides in the same consolidated pass. The rerun follows on the fix's verification under Ben's standing direction.

(2) **The decline is a good thing**: he saw he lacked information he needed and reached out — the knock on the door the office principle asks for — instead of bluffing a brief. **The ruling generalises his 2026-11-20 gate principle (allow or correct) to doorway refusals:** a refusal is a gate in the flow — the problem named in actionable form (what arrived, what's needed, an example), correction the designed path through it, the work continuing to completion on the other side. The doorway's authority does not soften: nothing dispatches until the call is right. What changes is the refusal's place in the flow — a working-through point, not a dead end the worker can only bounce off or decline against. Carried to Vane with the run record as the director's answer to the rerun's open question; the implementation scope is hers to examine.

477. **⭐⭐ BEN'S DEFINITION — THE BRIEFCASE IS A FOLDER, NOT A PROMPT; ITS CONTENTS ARE HIS (2026-11-24 18:55 MST; MEMORY.md): "if its in his briefcase (a folder hes allowed to utlilize, not a prompt) then he has access to it. its his breifcase."** The briefcase is a folder the worker is allowed to utilise — not material pasted into his prompt. Access to its contents is constitutive: decided once, when the office stocks the briefcase, never granted per call. The walls stand at the briefcase's edge — what isn't stocked isn't reachable; inside, no tollbooth on his own materials. Carriage bounds on pull results are plumbing that protects the worker's context (the attempt-6 lesson), not permission. The definition names the rerun's lockout as the design error it was: a worker refused his own case's contents through a shape rule on his only door. It governs the combined configuration's framing — the second door is not a grant of access to his own briefcase; it is his briefcase getting its other door — and goes to Vane in the package.
478. **⭐ BEN'S REFINEMENT — ACCESS MEANS UTILISE, NOT OVERWRITE (2026-11-24 18:56 MST; MEMORY.md): "access doesnt mean overwrite. it means utlilize."** The briefcase definition (INDEX 477) gains its second clause: the worker's access to his briefcase is utilise-only — he reads and works with its contents; he does not alter them. The stock stands as the office packed it (its integrity is the evidence boundary the brief is checked against); his own work lives in his scratch, which is his to write. The combined configuration's pull side already conforms (the retrieval family has no write against `sources/`; `sandbox.write` is scratch-scoped). The room side gains a rehearsal witness under the same instruction: a scripted attempt to overwrite a stocked source must fail or leave it byte-identical, its hash asserted after the attempt — and if the room currently permits the overwrite, that is reported as a finding with its mechanism, not quietly redesigned.
479. **⭐ BEN'S REMINDER — PULL REQUESTS FROM THE SHELF, THE GLOSSARY IN HIS BRIEFCASE (2026-11-24 18:58 MST): "Pull requests can be made by him from the shelf, he gets the glossery in his bc remember."** His own briefcase design of 2026-11-16, named back into the pull enablement: the shelf is the SOURCE and the briefcase is drawn from it per task (not a second store); pull requests are the worker's, made against the shelf; and the briefcase carries the GLOSSARY — the map of what is pullable, addresses plus the task's vocabulary bound to the shelf's — because an agent cannot request what it cannot conceive of. Carried into the combined-configuration build the same hour: the run's briefcase must carry the glossary as a distinct component (the three sources by address, one line each, plus his scratch as his writing space), and the rehearsal's scripted pulls must be formed from the glossary, each citing the entry it answers to. If the package construction cannot carry a glossary component, that is reported as a finding, not improvised around.
