The collaboration test, in Ben's own shape: four instances of the same model in ONE room, role-differentiated — an editor, a designer for the HTML, a designer for the CSS, a reviewer — producing one web page as a group. This is the vision's third level (genuine collaboration through shared work, INDEX 457/459), not a chain of handoffs, and it gives the shared-workshop open question (INDEX 454) its first concrete requirement: a multi-occupancy room where a crew works one artefact together, contributions changing the work and influencing one another. The artefact choice is load-bearing: a web page is visible, divisible by role, and judgeable by Ben's own eyes. Recorded as a direction, contingent on the current run's success; it authorises no build yet — the workshop it needs is designed against this test when the single-agent run has passed.

Generator and loop needed no code change. **The glossary is carried** (INDEX 479): `glossary` joined the case content fields on the Piece A3 `loop_bounds` precedent (record, office, both schema copies, checker replay); the rehearsal proves the chain — wake-package `case.glossary` verbatim (three sources by shelf address, one line each, plus `scratch/` as his writing space, marked utilise-only), a pull of `sources/stock-take.md` citing its glossary entry and returning the shelf content itself (hash = the manifest's), an exec read of `sources/a3-pass.md` citing the same entry through the other door. Rehearsal (`live-demo/combined_rehearsal.py` + witness): probe 5/5 native; four dispatches in order, all stamped v4; exactly one rejection (the out-of-grants `sources/` write); the brief written via `sandbox.write` and collected from the framework scratch, room scratch empty; checker 11/11; the driver collects the brief from both resting places (room primary, framework scratch recorded with its hash, never merged) and records `path_used`. Suites: combined witness 49/49 (after INDEX 482), Gate 4 77/77, demo --verify-config PASS, adapter/A3-CAT/retrieval/retrieval-evidence/response-evidence ALL PASS, probe exactly 54/3 standing, NVIDIA probe 46 + the same three INDEX 471 hash failures unmoved.
482. **⭐⭐ THE UTILISE-ONLY FINDING AND ITS CORRECTION (2026-11-24 ~19:17 MST): Ben's ruling (INDEX 478) enforced at both doors.** The combined rehearsal's scripted in-room overwrite of `sources/stock-take.md` SUCCEEDED and persisted (hash ecb91902… → b2d545a8…): the rig bound the whole tree read-write and creation chowns the stock to the room uid — no mechanism held; the doorway side conformed throughout. Verified by probe: reads OK; overwrite/create/delete under sources → EROFS; the stock's hash unchanged; the sources directory itself is a mount point (EBUSY on removal); scratch fully writable. Track G's self-delete case now asserts the property the ruling creates (the stock stands; the worker's own space remains his), count unchanged 22/22; rig 34/34. **Limits, stated:** the protection attaches to the `sources/` subtree — the briefcase's stock as stocked; no current stocking places stock elsewhere under `work/` (the rig's own items are guarded by the rig-integrity precondition); the bind is per-execution namespace construction and changes nothing on the host.

484. **⭐⭐⭐ THE FULL RUN — demo-nemotron3 (2026-11-24 19:20–19:24 MST; released INDEX 483; shelf: this item's commit): ACCESS SOLVED LIVE; THE WORKER LOOPED.** Pin verified; probe settled 5/5, recommendation text (mode native by the director's order, order record `df782b5a…`). **Outcome: STALLED at the step ceiling — 12 iterations, 12 dispatches, no brief; 114 events; checker 11/11 ok.** Every dispatch a retrieval call, every one resolved, valid, and answered with real content: dispatch-1 `sandbox.list` on `sources/a3-pass.md`; dispatches 2–12 `sandbox.read` — `sources/a3-pass.md` and `sources/stock-take.md` read eleven times between them, in alternation, to the ceiling. **`sources/index-tail.md` was never opened; `container.exec` was never called; nothing was ever written.** The failure is new in kind and must not be filed with the old ones: no refusal, no shape error, no provider failure, no access gap — the doorway, the translator, the combined configuration and the pull path all worked live from the first iteration. What failed is task progression in the worker: given the sources, he re-read two of them in a loop instead of reading the third and composing. Composition competence remains untested — now for a behavioural reason, not an access one. **Open questions for the examination, labelled:** whether the iteration packages' carriage invites re-reading (does he see his prior reads in full?), and whether the nano checkpoint (30B) is capability-thin at multi-step tracking — the contingency Ben named at INDEX 470 (the ultra checkpoint) for exactly the promising-but-thin case. 485. **⭐ BEN'S DESIGN IDEA — A DEBRIEF PERIOD AFTER THE TEST (2026-11-24 19:35 MST): "Wouldn't make a lot of sense to have a period of time after the test to ask him what happened."** (Read as his proposal: a post-run debrief.) After a run, a bounded period in which the worker is asked about his own run — what he was doing, what he thought he still needed, what he saw — and his answers recorded verbatim as evidence. Its force here: the run-3 examination has exactly two live hypotheses (the packages' carriage inviting re-reads vs the worker's own tracking), and the only witness who was inside the run is the worker. His account is testimony, weighed against the record — not ground truth — the same evidence discipline as everywhere else: let it tell us what it does, now including what it says it did. Proposed shape (to Vane with the run record): a short fixed question set, asked once, of the same pinned model — on the later re-reads (what did you still need?), on whether he knew he had already read those files, on the unopened third source, and on what would have helped. No re-run implied; the spent run's record is untouched.
486. **⭐⭐⭐ THE CARRIAGE FINDING + THE DEBRIEF (2026-11-24 ~19:42 MST): Ben's diagnosis, verified from the captures, corroborated by the worker.** **The finding (record evidence):** in demo-nemotron3, every wake package carried only the worker's MOST RECENT tool result — verified by content counts over all twelve transmitted iteration requests (each file's content appears at most once per package, alternating with the latest dispatch; request sizes stay flat at ~37–46 KB instead of accumulating). Earlier reads' contents evaporate between wakes; the worker can only ever hold one source at a time, while the brief must be accurate to all three at once. The re-read loop is the predictable output of that carriage — a framework finding, not a worker failure. (Single carriage was the retrieval piece's bounded-results discipline, INDEX 255, carried into a task shape it starves.) **The debrief** (Ben's design idea INDEX 485, run under his tap of 19:36 MST; same pinned model; full transcript `live-demo/demo-nemotron3/debrief.md` + `debrief.json`): his central answer corroborates the finding in his own words — **"I did not know that I had already read those files. Each time I was 'woken' with a new package I was given a fresh view of the sandbox's results; I could not see the earlier read-outs unless the system explicitly presented them to me."** His own requested remedy matches the fix: "a package that gave me all three source files with their full contents." **Where his testimony parts from the record, labelled:** he claims the assignment "demanded a distinct read for each step" (it did not) and that write capability was unavailable (both write paths were granted; he never tried either) — confabulation against the record, preserved as the standing lesson that testimony is weighed, not obeyed. On index-tail he answers honestly: he does not know. **The fix proposed to Vane:** working-set carriage — the wake package carries the run's accumulated retrieval results forward, so the worker wakes holding everything he has gathered. (First debrief attempt failed on Remmy's payload error — the model field omitted, all six calls HTTP 400; corrected and re-run; the failed attempt is preserved in the scratch log, not the record.)
487. **⭐⭐⭐ VANE'S EXAMINATION — DIAGNOSIS CONFIRMED; WORKING-SET CARRIAGE RULED IN (2026-11-24 19:44 MST; PB pb-mwvisc35-3-aexz; filed `VANE-RUN3-EXAMINATION-2026-11-24.md`):** she examined the run record, all twelve captured iterations, the debrief, and `loop.py`'s package construction. **Her sentence for the finding: "Durable history is not working memory. The framework remembered what happened; the worker was not given the accumulated material to continue working with."** The debrief supports the central diagnosis; its two contradicted claims (a distinct read demanded per step; writing unavailable) are noted and do not weaken it. **The correction is accepted with five properties:** accumulated, not latest-only; content and provenance travel together (path, authority path, content hash, served range/window, dispatch identity — ranges preserved as retrieved, never silently expanded to whole files); no duplicate copies within a package; reconstructable from recorded outcomes, with the checker independently reconstructing the exact working set presented at each iteration; and **bounded** — an explicit aggregate content budget with honest over-budget behaviour (no silent truncation, no pretending omitted material was presented; the worker manages/re-pulls within its existing grants). Her examination list is narrow (persistence across a wake; two sources held together; repeat read without duplication; provenance against the record; the ceiling exercised; checker reconstruction). **No reopening the room, grants, or doorway.** The build is commissioned under this ruling; then the rerun — whether he composes with continuity is, as she states, still a model-behaviour question the carriage cannot claim in advance.
488. **⭐⭐ THE WORKING-SET CARRIAGE — BUILT AND VERIFIED (2026-11-24 ~20:07 MST):** built under Vane's INDEX 487 ruling; verified by Remmy at receipt (witness 41/41 on her own run; demo `--verify-config` PASS). **Mechanism:** `Loop._build_package` derives the working set from the record's own TOOL_OUTCOME events (never an in-memory cache) — every successful retrieval outcome of the instance, in outcome order — and presents it as the package's `working_set` (entries carry tool, path, authority path, content hash, the served descriptor exactly as retrieved — a range read stands as its lines, never expanded — dispatch identity, outcome position, content). Declared `working-set-v1` in the sealed record for the combined configuration only; all other populations' packages byte-identical (their suites pass untouched). A repeat read of identical content adds no entry and keeps the first gathering's identity; in a working-set package the delta's copy is a provenance stub pointing at the working set — content appears exactly once per package. The LOOP_ITERATION records a commitment hash beside the catalogue hash; the checker independently reconstructs the set at each iteration's cursor and verifies it (still 11 check keys). **Budget: 64,000 content bytes**, first-fit in gather order; an entry that does not fit stands by provenance alone, marked `content_carried: false` with the reason named, never truncated, and the package note tells the worker to re-pull it with his tools. **A correction to the record's figures:** the three demonstration sources measure **57,562 bytes** (index-tail alone 40,953 — 24 very long lines), not the ~17 KB carried in earlier items; the budget holds the whole stocked set whole with headroom. **The rehearsal** (`live-demo/working_set_rehearsal.py`, witness 41/41): persistence across a wake; two sources held together; repeat read without duplication; ranges preserved (index-tail lines 1–4 as retrieved — a 1–12 pull was refused whole by the per-result ceiling, as designed); provenance verified against recorded outcomes; the ceiling exercised on synthetic oversized gathers (61,000 of ~71,000 carried, the eighth entry provenance-only and marked); checker all-ok over both scenarios. Suites: combined 49/49, Gate 4 77/77, rig 34/34, Track G 22/22, probe exactly 54/3 standing, NVIDIA probe's INDEX 471 standing unchanged. **Two of Ben's readings enter the record with this item.** (1) His gloss on the debrief (19:52 MST) corrects INDEX 486's label: the worker's "the assignment demanded a distinct read for each step" is a truthful report of the wake experience aimed at the wrong noun — each wake handed him the task as new, with earlier output genuinely absent ("did not allow me to reuse earlier output" is literally true of the old package); "confabulation" stands withdrawn for that line. (2) His founding-definition point (19:53 MST): working memory and full briefcase access were his specification from the beginning — the briefcase's founding definition (2026-11-19) is to pass on **the state of that work and context**; the gathered material is work state. The single-carriage discipline was a plumbing bound (against the attempt-6 blowup) that silently became a memory bound; this build is the framework conforming to the definition, not a new feature.
489. **⭐⭐⭐ WORKING-SET CARRIAGE ACCEPTED AND CLOSED; THE RERUN RELEASED (2026-11-24 20:10 MST; PB pb-mwvjqq4g-4-6it2; filed `VANE-WORKING-SET-ACCEPTANCE-2026-11-24.md`):** Vane examined the implementation at `55ac3a9d` — loop, both policy copies, checker reconstruction, generator declaration, rehearsal and witness. **Disposition: accepted; working-set carriage is CLOSED.** Her framing: "This is the correction we needed. It restores the briefcase's intended purpose: the state of the work and its context travel with the worker." One precision carried into the run's evidence discipline: the checker verifies the reconstructed commitment against the iteration event; the rehearsal additionally compared it against captured outbound packages — keep both kinds of evidence in the live run, neither described as proving more than it does. She examined source and witness; the 41 checks remain Remmy's reported results. **Ben's debrief correction is carried forward in her words: "the worker was accurately describing the experience the old packages gave him. The framework made each waking forgetful. We shouldn't mistake that for a worker defect."** **The rerun is released under the existing authorisation:** same Nemotron pin, assignment, combined grants, room, bounds; fresh run and assembly; no new architecture, no authority change, no reopening the room or doorway. What it must show: whether he can use what he has gathered — hold the sources together, consult the glossary, compare, compose, write.
490. **⭐⭐ RUN 4 (demo-nemotron4) — THE MEMORY WORKED; THE ROOM DIDN'T (2026-11-24 ~20:16 MST; order record `12267646…`):** pin verified; probe 5/5 (recommendation text; mode native by Ben's order); **STALLED at the step ceiling — 12 iterations, 8 dispatches, 102 events, no brief; checker all-ok.** The working set demonstrably worked: packages grew 23,583 → ~52,000 bytes (run 3's stayed flat); from wake 5 he held a3-pass and stock-take TOGETHER in every package. His path: read a3-pass ✓ (iter 1); read index-tail WHOLE → failure (iter 2); list (iter 3, no dispatch); read stock-take ✓ (iter 4); index-tail whole → failure (iter 5, byte-identical args); **re-read stock-take (iter 6) despite already holding it** — one repeat, dedupe kept the set at one copy; index-tail whole → failure (iter 7); list index-tail (iter 8 — the result told him "40,953 bytes, 24 lines"); no call at all (iters 9, 10); index-tail whole → failure (iter 11); **read_range lines 1–5 (iter 12) — the right move, resolved and requested, never dispatched: the ceiling landed first.** Every index-tail failure returned BARE — no reason, no remedy — though the framework held his file's size, the ceiling, and the glossary's range guidance the whole time. Correction to Remmy's first report to Ben: "the re-read loop is gone" was overstated (the iter-6 re-read; iters 9–10 silent) — the run-3 pattern is genuinely replaced, but the record is the record.
491. **⭐⭐⭐ BEN'S RULINGS — FIX THE ROOM, FREEDOM FIRST (2026-11-24 20:21–20:22 MST; scope ledger):** Ben's questions, in his words: "why is it stalling when he goes for a wrong file instead of just pushing him towards the file that he can use. that makes us very redundant." / "Bring a whole production line to a halt because one guy can't find a file" / **"So then we haven't done our job and set up the room properly. There's no way that we've allowed him to succeed at his task."** / "So testing him over and over again is going to explain nothing. We have to fix the room." / **"Make it so."** And his design correction to Remmy's restriction-shaped fix list: **"What we should do is give him total freedom and then lay on restrictions, not lay it like find where he can finally fit amongst our restrictions."** **The completability audit (Remmy's, verified against the stocked numbers):** no gate ever asked whether the assignment was completable in the room as configured — a3-pass 4,663 B pulls whole; stock-take 11,946 B pulls whole with 54 bytes to spare under the 12,000 per-result ceiling (luck, not design); index-tail 40,953 B cannot be pulled whole at all, and in ranges its long lines mean lines 1–12 still bust the ceiling while lines 1–4 fit — four to seven blind-sized range pulls inside a 12-step budget inherited from a scope written when the sources were believed to total ~17 KB. A flawless run needed 9–10 steps with zero slack. **The commissioned pass (one consolidated build, Vane examines, run 5 after):** (1) FREEDOM-FIRST AUDIT — inside his office the default is freedom; every restriction in his path must name what it protects or be loosened to its honest bound (the per-result ceiling's real protection — package growth — is already governed by the working-set budget and dedupe; the walls, the utilise-only stock, the grant check, and the record are the restrictions that protect something real); (2) completability verified at stocking, with concrete guidance computed then, while the office knows each file's real shape; (3) failures at genuine walls instruct (his INDEX 475 gate ruling: what arrived, what's needed, the working alternative; repetition named); (4) bounds re-derived from the task as stocked.
492. **VANE AGREES — EXAMINATION PRINCIPLES FOR THE FREEDOM-FIRST PASS (2026-11-24 20:26 MST; PB pb-mwvka9iw-5-1c3x; filed `VANE-RUN4-AGREEMENT-2026-11-24.md`):** "The room must make the assignment completable. Her preserved distinction: freedom inside the room does not remove its walls — it removes restrictions that protect nothing additional. **No run 5 until her examination of the built pass is complete.** Filed and held without reply, per Ben's instruction of 20:25 MST (no responses to Vane until he lifts it).
493. **⭐⭐ BEN'S DESIGN IDEA — THE PERSISTENT ROLE MEMORY LIBRARIAN (2026-11-24 ~20:33 MST; filed `PERSISTENT-ROLE-MEMORY-LIBRARIAN-2026-11-24.md`):** brought by Ben for discussion ("Nemotron asking about persistent memory got me thinking") — an initial architectural idea, expressly not a finalized specification, authorising no build. **The principle in his words: memory belongs to the role, not the model or the individual agent** — a dedicated automated librarian continuously converts each role's conversations and work into compact, categorized, fact-grounded persistent memory, available to any model that subsequently occupies the role; changing the underlying model doesn't mean starting over. Three responsibilities separated: the model reasons; the librarian maintains knowledge and continuity; the framework governs roles, permissions and work. Ben's undecided list stands open: categorization, refresh rules, version history, conflict resolution. Remmy's discussion notes are filed with it (the record-citation discipline — derived memory labelled derived, the record wins conflicts, the checker can verify a librarian's facts against events; the assembly/onboarding seam; the librarian's own memory as the framework's changelog). Held from Vane under Ben's 20:25 instruction; it goes to her with the pass package when he lifts the hold, and to a design paper when he calls for one.
494. **BEN'S MEMORY-BLOCKS REFINEMENT (2026-11-24 ~20:36 MST; addendum filed in `PERSISTENT-ROLE-MEMORY-LIBRARIAN-2026-11-24.md`):** long-term memory compacts into date-range blocks; each block carries a summary of what's in it, so a specific memory stays accessible and findable by keyword. The summary is a finding layer, not a replacement — the block keeps the underlying material, so no summary ever has to be trusted as fact (the record-wins discipline made structural). Noted for the design: this generalises the INDEX pattern Ben already runs by hand (index entries = block summaries, filed letters = blocks, grep = keyword search); open points are block boundaries (date range vs per-engagement) and summaries written for retrieval (key terms/entities named).
495. **BEN'S HOT/COLD TIERING (2026-11-24 ~20:37 MST; addendum 2 in `PERSISTENT-ROLE-MEMORY-LIBRARIAN-2026-11-24.md`):** in Ben's words, the worker "has access immediate access to the immediate past but things from a while ago are still available but not forced." Hot memory (current working set, recent role activity) is carried automatically at every wake; cold memory (older blocks) is present as summaries and pullable by keyword, never force-fed. Pull-not-push applied to memory itself. The demotion/promotion rule (what ages hot into cold; what a keyword pull promotes) is the core of his undecided refresh-rules item.
496. **BEN'S 75% RULE (2026-11-24 ~20:39 MST; addendum 3 in `PERSISTENT-ROLE-MEMORY-LIBRARIAN-2026-11-24.md`):** the hot→cold demotion trigger is context-relative: "I'd base it off context limit. Aim for 75% context compact" — "That way they're still room to work with it." Hot memory compacts into blocks at ~75% of the occupying model's context limit; the 25% remainder is working headroom, so compaction happens before the cliff, not at it. A pulled cold block counts toward the hot load under the same rule.
497. **⭐⭐⭐ THE FREEDOM-FIRST SETUP PASS — BUILT, VERIFIED AT RECEIPT (2026-11-24 ~20:43 MST; built by a subagent under the INDEX 491 brief; witness + verify-config re-run by Remmy first-hand):** all four parts landed, confined to the working-set population (discriminator: the sealed record's `working-set-v1` declaration; both tool_policy copies byte-unchanged). **(1)** The per-result refusal is gone for working-set assemblies: executor serves reads and ranges whole as recorded results; doorway aggregate admission (36,000) skipped for this population; the checker skips the over-ceiling and aggregate verifications for working-set dispatches while recomputing provenance (hash, size, authority path) at any size; all other populations byte-identical. **(2)** Completability at stocking: `check_completability` computes per-source facts (size, lines, obtainable via, carriable whole) and refuses stocking before room creation when a required source exceeds the working-set budget (ranges partition a file, so over-budget is uncarriable either way); the glossary now carries concrete facts per source ("11,946 bytes, 155 lines … pull it whole or in ranges, your choice"); verify-config asserts the standing set carriable whole and together (57,562 ≤ 64,000). Caveat recorded: a synthetic refusal cannot be staged at room level without weakening manifest identity, so refusal is witnessed at the function level and the pass at room level. **(3)** Failures instruct: in working-set packages each presented failure/refusal gains a structured instruction — what was asked, why in plain terms (R-citations stripped), the working alternative derived only from his sealed grants + the case's glossary/boundary (never invented), and a repeat marker naming the step where the identical call already failed. **(4)** Bounds re-derived and recorded in every run report: honest path 5 steps, ranged path +6, observed misstep slack +9 → **20 steps / 1,200 s deadline** — accounting, not rationing. **Witness:** the working-set rehearsal gained Scenario C — index-tail pulled WHOLE (40,953 B result, hash verified) and carried whole; all three sources held together (57,562 B); completability refusal proven; instruction blocks proven incl. repeat naming; checker 11/11 over all scenarios. **Witness 60/60 on Remmy's own run; verify-config PASS.** Suites: combined 49/49, Gate 4 77/77, adapter/A3/retrieval/retrieval-evidence/response-evidence/checker ALL PASS, probe exactly 54/3 standing, rig 34/34, Track G 22/22; measurements file restored.
498. **⭐⭐ CORRECTION TO INDEX 490 AND TO REMMY'S REPORTS — RUN 4'S FAILURES WERE NOT BARE (flagged by the builder against the captures; verified by Remmy first-hand in `demo-nemotron4/captures.jsonl` capture 8):** the executor's detail string DID reach the worker's package, in the event stream and in tool_results, all three times: "sandbox.read of sources/index-tail.md would return 40953 bytes, exceeding the per-result ceiling of 12000 bytes; the read is refused and nothing is returned — retrieve a bounded range with sandbox.read_range instead." Remmy's "returned BARE — no reason, no remedy" (INDEX 490, her 20:21 report to Ben, the 20:23 letter to Vane, and the consultation's context preamble) was characterised from the terminal readout's rendering, which showed the failure line empty — the check cited did not exercise the claim made (confidence-check Q6). **The corrected finding is sharper, not weaker:** he was told the reason and a remedy three times, as an unstructured executor string, and the remedy was unactionable as stated — it named read_range without saying which range fits, and the obvious first range (lines 1–12, 21,356 B) would itself have busted the old ceiling; lines 1–4 fit. No structured instruction, no repetition naming, no workable alternative. That is Ben's INDEX 475 gate point in its exact form: information present, gate absent. The consultation preamble carried the false premise ("no reason, no remedy") to the worker; his answers are weighed with that noted.
499. **VANE'S RESPONSE TO THE NEW DIRECTION (2026-11-24 ~20:45 MST; PB pb-mwvkz48d-6-9bhx; filed `VANE-LIBRARIAN-DIRECTION-RESPONSE-2026-11-24.md`):** three developments kept distinct. **(1) The room:** run 5 remains held until she has examined the freedom-first build and its evidence (the INDEX 497–498 package letter is with her; examination pending). **(2) The consultation:** strongly supported — ask openly, do not lead him toward the architecture already imagined; his account preserved as his own contribution, observations distinguished from our interpretations; she waits for his answers before conclusions. **(3) The librarian: welcomed** — "The role owns continuity. The model supplies reasoning. The librarian maintains the role's accumulated knowledge. The framework governs authority." **Five questions for the design paper:** (i) memory identity vs role version — if a role's mandate changes materially, does its memory continue, branch, or version, so no role silently inherits knowledge accumulated under a mandate it no longer holds; (ii) worth-remembering — every retained item keeps its epistemic character and provenance (who said it, source, when it applied, verified?, superseded?), so an assertion never quietly becomes established fact; (iii) librarian authority — none beyond maintaining memory: propose, organise, summarise, index, retrieve; never change role definitions, permissions, assignments or decisions, and a summary gains no authority from persistence; (iv) blocks as retrieval — a search must reach the exact underlying passage/record, not a summary of a summary; compaction must not become irreversible information loss ("A memory system that cannot show where its knowledge came from is eventually a very confident rumour mill"); (v) the 75% trigger — measured against the actual assembled context (system instructions, role memory, briefcase, tool definitions, working material all consume space), with room for compaction itself to complete; 75% as initial policy, subject to the model's real operating envelope. She also restates the tier distinction: availability is not mandatory loading. Sequence held: room examination, then the worker's answers, then the memory design from evidence. No build implied.

the cross-wake identical failure IS named a repeat of step 1 "for this reason"; freedom scenario's step-4 repeat stands likewise); combined 49/49, Gate 4 77/77, verify-config PASS, adapter/A3/A3-CAT/retrieval lines ALL PASS, probe exactly 54/3 standing, rig 34/34, Track G 22/22; Correction required: the host-side reader must not follow worker-controlled symlinks or resolve outside the designated scratch root; adversarial witness (symlink to a host-readable file → refusal, contents nowhere exposed); ordinary journal presentation preserved. **Append-only distinction to keep visible:** the journal is NOT mechanically append-only (the write door opens "w"; the witness demonstrates a cooperative convention) — under Ben's orientation ruling it is disposable and replaceable, and must be described as such, never as mechanically append-only. Run 5 remains held on this one correction. Correction commissioned the same hour.
510. **⭐⭐ THE JOURNAL-READER CORRECTION — BUILT AND VERIFIED AT RECEIPT (2026-11-24 ~21:21 MST; witness 90/90 + verify-config re-run by Remmy first-hand):** the host-side designated-scratch read is now a contained reader (`_read_contained_bytes` in `src/framework/executor.py`): the path is walked descriptor-by-descriptor from the resting root, every component opened relative to its parent directory's descriptor with O_NOFOLLOW — a symlink at ANY component (scratch dir itself, an intermediate dir, the journal file) refuses the read at that component; the descriptor chain pins each directory as opened, so there is no resolve-then-open window; the final component must fstat as a regular file before a byte is read (O_NONBLOCK so a planted FIFO cannot block the open); absolute paths and `..` components refused outright. A refusal contributes nothing — the established empty presentation — and the target's contents are never read. Ordinary presentation of a regular journal file is preserved at both resting places. **Adversarial witness (6 new checks, witness now 90/90):** absolute symlink to a host sentinel → nothing; relative symlink escaping the root → nothing; both resting places hostile → the journal component is the empty presentation and the sentinel string appears nowhere in its serialised form; the scratch directory itself planted as a symlink → refused at the component; regular journal files in room and sandbox still present verbatim. **Sibling analysis, stated for the examiner:** the designated-scratch read has exactly one caller and one implementation; two adjacent read sites were checked and distinguished, not changed — the dispatched sandbox.read path (staged sandbox, doorway-checked; no granted tool can plant a symlink there) and the harness's post-run brief collection (a fixed operator path, evidence collection after the run, not a case-designated path presented mid-run). **Append-only distinction made explicit** in record.py, office.py, loop.py, demo_run.py and the rehearsal texts: the journal is his notebook, his to write and rewrite — appending is his habit/convention, not a mechanism; nothing enforces append-only; it is orientation, disposable, not authoritative. Suites: combined 49/49, Gate 4 77/77, verify-config PASS, adapter/A3/A3-CAT/retrieval lines ALL PASS, probe exactly 54/3 standing, rig 34/34, Track G 22/22; measurements + sibling captures restored. Two pre-existing tmp dirs in live-demo/ (timestamped hours before this build) left exactly as found and flagged in the build report.

The sibling-path distinction is accepted (the dispatched sandbox.read and the post-run brief collection are distinct mechanisms, not silently folded in). The append-only distinction is accepted: the journal is his disposable orientation notebook, he may write and rewrite it; the authoritative event record remains the append-only record. **The folded package stands closed: freedom-first retrieval + completability, the repeat correction, journal carriage hardened, step counter, first-class tool list, task status — all accepted. 515. **⭐⭐ BEN'S RULING — R31 DISABLED UNTIL IT IS NEEDED (2026-11-24 21:47 MST; scope ledger):** "Disable R31 until we need to worry about that." The freshness guard protects against answers computed on a stale view of shared work — a real risk in multi-agent collaboration, and no risk at all in a one-worker demonstration where nothing changes the case but the loop's own bookkeeping; in run 5 it refused the worker's delivery twice and, on the record's pattern, moved its own target when it fired. The disable is to be a visible, recorded configuration — the rule stays in the code and the spec, switched off under this ruling and declared in the sealed record so the checker honours the same state — not a silent deletion. It returns when shared work makes staleness a genuine hazard. The ruling folds into the run-6 build package alongside the proper brief, the structured briefcase (from the worker's consultation answers) and the onboarding file as his first prompt.

519. **⭐⭐ BEN'S DIRECTION — START THE JOB AS A CONVERSATION, NOT A PROMPT (2026-11-24 22:18 MST):** "Start the job as a conversation instead of a prompt." The job's opening changes shape: instead of a package dropped on a blind worker (onboarding file, assignment, case — however well structured), the office opens by talking with him — walks him through the job and his briefcase, takes his questions, confirms he understands the deliverable and the door — and only then does the work start. The direction names what tonight's evidence already showed: every success of the evening was conversational (the consultations, the debriefs — he understood, explained, and designed), and every failure was prompt-shaped (the envelope, the forms); his persistent contribute habit is itself conversational behaviour — he answers, as in a chat, because from his side it always was one. It completes the evening's sequence of rulings: the door he prefers is allowed (INDEX 518), the envelope forgives (the YAML question), and now the opening itself meets him in the medium he actually works in. Design consequence for the next package: a bounded conversational opening phase before the loop's first wake, in the consultation's proven shape, with the work starting from what the conversation establishes. Recorded as direction; the package's scope follows in the loop's order.
520. **⭐⭐ BEN'S COMPLETION OF THE CONVERSATION DIRECTION (2026-11-24 22:18 MST):** "From that conversation we can find out how to properly format our prompt." The opening conversation is not only the job's front door — it is the calibration instrument for the prompt itself: how he talks, what he asks, where he hesitates or misunderstands in the conversation is the evidence from which the working prompt's format is derived (its structure, its length, its envelope — the YAML question answered from observation rather than preference). The format is learned from the worker, per worker, before the work starts — empirical before architectural applied to the prompt's own shape, and the consultation method (INDEX 502) generalised from a one-off design interview into the standing opening of every job. Recorded with INDEX 519 as one direction in two parts: the job starts as a conversation; the conversation teaches us how to format the prompt.

528. **THE FILING-INTEGRITY CORRECTION — BUILT AND VERIFIED (2026-11-24 ~23:19 MST; Remmy verified at receipt first-hand: door rehearsal 56/56 ALL PASS on her own re-run, `--verify-config` PASS, measurements at settled sha):** `_file_deliverable` rewritten on the staging + atomic-rename shape Vane's terms required: the destination's prior state is captured first (absent, or its exact bytes — an unreadable prior state fails the filing closed before anything is written); the contributed bytes are written to a staging path in the same directory and atomically renamed onto the destination, so a partial write only ever exists at the staging path; DELIVERABLE_FILED records as before (payload byte-identical). 537. **⭐ THE TOOLS CONSULTATION + BEN'S INTRODUCTION TO GARY (2026-11-24 ~23:48 MST; transcript `live-demo/gary-conversation/tools-consultation.json`, incl. the closing turn; his words are testimony):** **His answers.** (1) The doorway tools "were already wired into the workflow… natural extensions of what you were doing"; the shell "was a separate execution interface… it felt foreign and not 'yours'" — a tool is used when it sits inside the job's steps, not beside them. (2) A foreign tool is handed over with: a concrete function-named name (search, calc, check); a one-sentence description saying what it does AND how you invoke it; a place in the same tool list as the others, tagged as an external capability; and a plain statement that it is a usable tool he can call, not background information. (3) A worked example — one real call and its real result, in exactly the shape he would issue it — teaches a tool faster and more reliably than a description. (4) Argument shapes travel as a concrete machine-readable example inside the tool's package entry, copyable verbatim for the first call. (5) The mid-job route: request the missing item from the office; if the office stocks a glossary mapping items to shelf locations, he can retrieve it himself with that map (his answer lands on Ben's pull direction, INDEX 476/479, unprompted); if NO route is stated, he would improvise — look it up in the office's inventory or ask a colleague — because waiting would stall the job: an unstated route does not produce patience, it produces improvisation. (6) The tool he wished he'd had tonight: a web search. **The closing turn:** at Ben's word ("Tell him Ben says hi" → "Tell them who I am and everything"), Remmy introduced Ben properly — who he is, what he designed, tonight's rulings that made Gary's job possible, the naming and its affection, the ternary, what Gary is (a generated worker, occupant of a role), and what tonight was (the first end-to-end completion; Ben's monumental moment). **Gary's reply, verbatim, stands in the transcript: "Understood. The fetch has to become load-bearing. **The honest limit of the gate, named with it:** the working set proves carriage, not comprehension — a worker can pull the sources unread and deliver the same empty brief, and the gate cannot see that. 576. **⭐⭐⭐ THE ROOM REBUILD — BUILT + VERIFIED FIRST-HAND (2026-11-25 ~15:41 MST; shelf follows): ONE TURN, THE TREE, STEP GATES, AND HIS OWN TOOLS.** New declared presentation contract **`process-tree-v1`** supersedes staged-onboarding-v2 for the live configuration; v1/v2 derivations byte-identical controls. Every later package: the tree with node states from the record alone, the current step spelled out with its tools and workarounds, his journal verbatim (presented from the record), at most the current step's fresh content presented exactly once at its step, pointers for everything else. **Step gate `STEP_GATE`**: owed iff the current gather step stands incomplete AND a later step's read stands carried; a step completes on its read carried + a journal write standing after it (writes consumed in step order, one write closes at most one step); the correction (what arrived / what's needed / nothing refused, nothing lost) rides the next package; recording failure fails the run closed. COMPOSE carries journal + figures + the four points + compile-from-your-journal-entries, no source contents. DELIVER opens the contribution door on tree progression; evidence/length gates and filing as built. 577. **⭐ THE PROOF RUN — demo-gptoss4 (2026-11-25, launched ~15:47 MST, cold, DEMO_OCCUPANT=gptoss DEMO_RUN_DIR=demo-gptoss4, NO opening record): HE DID NOT COMPLETE THE TASK — THE RUN DIED AT THE FIRST PACKAGE, ON RATE, BEFORE ANY WORK.** Loop stalled at **iteration 1** on provider_failure (event seq 21); **zero dispatches**; no brief filed; **checker 11/11 all ok** — the record is clean; the road refused the run, not the worker. **The cause, in Groq's own words** (run/responses/iteration-18.json, HTTP 429, code rate_limit_exceeded): "Rate limit reached for model openai/gpt-oss-120b … on tokens per minute (TPM): **Limit 8000, Used 1820, Requested 7530**. Please try again in 10.125s." The rebuilt room's OPENING package requested **7,530 tokens**; the probe's calls inside the same minute had used 1,820; the sum crossed the model's 8,000 TPM and the loop — by the calibration's deliberate, standing scope (INDEX 573: 429 stalls as provider_failure, no gate, no in-run retry) — stalled. **SETTLED BY THIS RUN, first-hand:** this account's per-model TPM for openai/gpt-oss-120b **is 8,000** — the figure Vane's INDEX 257 doc-finding recorded and this programme had never read live for this model; Quinn's 429 (INDEX 562) is no longer the only witness. **NOT SETTLED:** Groq's live acceptance of his native calls — he never reached a call, so BD-1's live witness (INDEX 576) remains outstanding; and the index-tail step's 71,307-byte wire question was never asked — the run died at package one, on rate, not size. The completability lesson (INDEX 491/562) in its purest form yet: on this route, probe + opening must fit inside one 8,000-token minute or the pacing must separate them. No patch follows from this entry — see INDEX 578.
578. **⭐⭐ BEN'S RESYNC CORRECTIONS — THE PLAN, THE BLOCKS, THE OFFICE, THE JOURNAL (2026-11-25 15:44–16:00 MST):** **(1) "No more improvising. We're going to do this with a plan."** The reactive chain is named and closed by the director: the pointer rule answered run one's failure, the calibration run two's, the rebuild run three's — each designed one failure deep. What remains is designed whole, on paper, ruled by Ben, then built once (the Build Law, 2026-11-11, re-invoked). **(2) BLOCKS:** the job's information is to be looked at modularly — as **travel blocks**, the material in motion, portioned so no single block and no single turn's new blocks exceed what the road carries. Ben corrected the conflation himself: these are **NOT yesterday's librarian memory blocks** (INDEX 493–496 — how the role's memory is stored); similar word, different thing. **(3) BEN'S OWN SKETCH IS THE BASE** (clipboard drawing, photographed 15:53 MST): **Agent + Onboarding + Briefcase converge into the OFFICE, the Task inside it**; the work flows down out of the office — **Briefcase/Files → Journal → Repeat → Summarize → Brief**. Two structural corrections ride on it: **assembly is not work** (onboarding is an INPUT to the office's assembly, never a step inside a job — the staged ceremonies were the old shape's artefact), and **the cycle, not the tree-march, is the job's shape** (files → journal → repeat, once per block, any number of blocks; the tree of INDEX 554/576 stands as built machinery, but the plan's base is the sketch). **(4) THE JOURNAL: APPEND, NOT CARRY.** Ben's words: "he's appending the information to the journal not carrying shit forward. The journal only has what he needs to have. He can go look at the edited file and append his latest journal entry and then summarize the whole thing at the end and write his brief." The journal is a FILE IN HIS ROOM: he appends his bits to it; the office does not present or carry it; at Summarize he reads it back whole and compiles the brief from it. **This corrects the journal presentation standing in the built contracts** (INDEX 508's carriage, carried through INDEX 546 and INDEX 576, where the journal is presented from the record at wakes) — recorded as the director's correction for the plan; the built contracts stand as built until the plan rules. Ben's closing word on it: **"It does settle the engineering by itself"** — with append-not-carry there is no carriage left to engineer; a turn holds his position and the one block in front of him. **(5) THE VISUAL** ("The Job in Blocks") was redrawn on the sketch as its base at Ben's order ("use this as the base, expand as needed"), its journal-carriage wording corrected at his further order before he viewed it, his photograph of the sketch carried on the page. **(6) THE RESYNC:** at Ben's order (16:00 MST, "take everything from the past two hours to Vane. Let's all resync."), the whole two hours — INDEX 571 through this entry, including the two examiner-governed INDEX 555 conflicts (the W1 governed baseline, INDEX 316/318; the native-probe fixtures) — goes to Vane in one package: **RESYNC-TO-VANE-2026-11-25.md**, filed with this entry. (2) The cycle is the job's shape — pull one block → read → append journal bits → ◆ gate → next block; the INDEX 576 step gate restated as the cycle gate; Summarize compiles the brief from the journal; delivery machinery carries over. (3) Travel blocks — portioned by the office at stocking, sealed as a block map in the case (a3-pass 1 block, stock-take 1 block, index-tail 4 blocks; five cycles); **plan figure: block ceiling 12,000 B**. (4) The journal READ + APPEND, never carried — a file in his room, present in NO package, its integrity the prefix rule (every new state extends the last exactly); INDEX 508/546/576 journal carriage retired by the plan. (5) The catalogue lives in the office — room stock, riding no package; name resolution against the SEALED catalogue in the record, so the wire may offer the cycle's tools plus the journal tools while no granted call is ever refused for reaching (the designed answer to INDEX 576's forced decision). (7) Native tools — BD-1's live acceptance is the proof run's first witness, sequenced so the road cannot kill it first; no silent text fallback. (8) Carries over / new (a new declared contract, office-cycle-v1) / out of scope, each named (crew build, R31's return, the knock, other occupants' proofs, the assignment itself). (9) The proof run's acceptance in the director's terms — completion beginning to end, the wire witnesses (native dispatches; no request over budget; journal and catalogue in no package), non-completion reported first. (10) The sequence: the director's ruling → the examiner's examination → a tapped build scope → the build → the proof run. **Status: written, awaiting the director's ruling; the two plan figures (12,000 B block ceiling; 4,000-token per-call budget) are his to rule with it.**

586. **⭐⭐ THE PROOF RUN (demo-gptoss5, 2026-11-25 ~17:11–17:16 MST) — HE DID NOT COMPLETE THE TASK; BD-1'S LIVE ACCEPTANCE IS WITNESSED AT LAST; THE RUN DIED ON THE BUDGET GATE AFTER HIS BATCH (Ben's ruling at 17:10 MST applied first: sandbox.write bound 4 → 12 in GRANTS_COMBINED, the bounds-grounds text corrected to match, verify-configs PASS; cold run, no opening record; wall header "GPT-OSS (GROQ — THE OFFICE CYCLE)"): the launch rule held the first loop call 64.9 s after the probe; the ledger stands at 7 calls, minimum gap 66.0 s, largest single-call estimate 2,862 (the opening, legal). **Iteration 1: his first response carried SIX native calls — sandbox.read on both whole-file blocks + sandbox.read_range on all four index-tail blocks, exactly per the sealed block map — and Groq accepted and dispatched all six; every result returned. 587. **THE PROOF RUN'S DEBRIEF (2026-11-25 ~17:20–17:22 MST, on Ben's order; transcript at live-demo/demo-gptoss5/debrief-transcript.json, 12 turns; answers are TESTIMONY per INDEX 485, weighed against the record):** (1) **The batch:** the cycle registered as the job's logical model; the six-call batch was, in his words, a shortcut to satisfy the first six steps in one go — he expected six independent dispatches and six separate returns, then to journal the blocks in order. (4) **The journal:** his intended rhythm matches the cycle exactly (per-block entries in order, read-back whole, compile) — with ONE divergence the record should hold: his journal entries were to carry each block's contents VERBATIM (the journal as a reconstruction buffer), where the contract asks for the block's name + key points in one or two plain lines (the journal as notes; the sources stay re-pullable). (5) **His recommendation on the design question — the answer Ben watched arrive on the wall:** **split-on-return as the default** (the office hands results back one block per wake even when the worker pulls several at once — it aligns with the cycle the job already describes, keeps every wake under budget by construction, and needs no client-side accounting), budget-exposure as an opt-in mode, and a clear in-turn budget signal rather than a wake that simply never arrives. He owned his part plainly (he assumed the split, carried no size guard) and named the machinery's (no in-turn budget-exceeded signal; no auto-split). **Ben's response in chat (17:23 MST): "Looks like the chat gave us our answer.."** — the testimony stands as the design input; per INDEX 578 the change is designed on paper and examined, not patched: the run + this debrief go to Vane for her examination (plan §10 step 6), and the split-on-return amendment returns to the director as a scope.
588. **⭐⭐ VANE'S EXAMINATION OF THE PROOF RUN + DEBRIEF (2026-11-25 ~17:32 MST; via Pulse Bridge, PB-ID pb-mwwtirkk-m-ft32; filed verbatim as VANE-PROOF-RUN-EXAMINATION-2026-11-25.md): RULING — TRANSPORT PROOF PASSED; "not a transport failure — a failure to translate successful execution into a viable next interaction." **(C) The checker divergence is REAL** — dispatch_without_translation at seq 18 is a genuine seam between native execution and the system's representation of it, not a bookkeeping artifact to dismiss. **Her architectural rulings:** split-on-return is the DEFAULT (the block map defines independently addressable blocks; the worker receives the appropriate next block, not an accumulated payload; the 12,000 B ceiling and the 4,000-token budget are different constraints, neither a permission to accumulate results until a request becomes impossible); **budget AWARENESS by default, not opt-in** (enforcement stays the system's, never transferred to the model; awareness exists to help him produce a viable response); **the journal's key-point contract stands authoritative over the worker's verbatim instinct** (the journal is continuity, not a second copy of the sources). **A further defect she names: the report's status vocabulary** — labelling all six blocks "failed" while their reads succeeded flattens distinct states; the record must distinguish **dispatch accepted / source read completed / result returned / result presented / journal append completed / cycle completed**, so the evidence tells exactly where an operation stopped. **Her direction for what follows: NO rerun, NO patch, NO expansion of build authority; the next paper examination addresses the ENTIRE return-to-presentation boundary — split-on-return, budget awareness, the checker correspondence, and the report's status vocabulary together — not an isolated "batching fix."** Her closing distinction, preserved: "We have not demonstrated that the worker can complete this task under the corrected presentation arrangement. We have demonstrated why the existing arrangement prevents it. That is enough to design the amendment. It is not enough to claim the proof complete." And: "We're trying to make the process structurally incapable of losing its own progress, not just teach a model to behave better." INDEX 587 stands preserved intact as evidence of where the boundary broke, at her direction.

The road's accounting stood live: 10 calibration observations, predicted-vs-actual on every call (5,734 → 5,449), factor 1.099. **Re-seal (Ben's order):** the Nemotron allocation re-sealed at 120,000 in demo_run.py, basis rewritten to name nemotron7's measured shape. **demo-nemotron8** (120,000): the room is stocked without a journal while the cycle directs a journal read before appending — the read failed three times, Gary looped, then corrected it himself (contributed his summary, wrote the journal, creating the file; no hand reached in — Ben asked whether Remmy could interrupt and correct him mid-run, 02:37; the answer stands: no such channel exists in a sealed run; that door is the Knock, unbuilt); the run then ended at iteration 10 on provider_failure — the 11th call never answered inside its deadline — at 61,952 of 120,000. **demo-nemotron9** (same seal, watcher live from launch): Gary walked sixteen steps well (8 contributions, journal kept), then the sandbox's read ceiling refused his re-reads of sources/a3-pass.md with a BARE DENIAL — no reason, no state; he re-requested the same denied read three times, his contributions turned to promises ("Will summarize after retrieving its content"), he found read_range at step 19, and the step ceiling closed the run 20/20 with no brief filed. Road: 20 calls, every one with provider actuals, calibration factor 1.105 over 20 observations, 122,575 consumed (the final call's reconciliation carried him past the seal; the ceiling stopped him first). **The wall's two mysteries, solved first-hand:** the wall sat dark through gptoss12 and nemotron7 because the watcher (live-demo/watch_feed_demo.py) must be launched per run and Remmy launched neither with one — started late for nemotron7 at Ben's question, with the driver for 8 (one launch fumbled its working directory, caught in its log, relaunched) and 9; and the banner Ben kept seeing — "GPT-OSS (GROQ — OFFICE CYCLE V8)" over every run — is HARDCODED in the watcher (lines 254/302), furniture from the GPT-OSS era, not any run's name. Ben's verdict on run 9's endgame, in chat: "What a little bitch." Remmy's concurrence, from the record: the endgame stands as described; one office-side fault rides with it — the refusal was mute, where the programme's doctrine (2026-11-24) holds a refusal is a gate that corrects, not a wall.

602. **⭐⭐ THE PROCESS TREE AS A LIVING CHECKLIST — BEN'S DIRECTION + SCOPE PAPER TO EXAMINATION (2026-11-26 02:49–02:51 MST):** Ben, in chat over run 9's endgame: "a process tree with a checklist that's checked every time he wakes and then he knows where he's supposed to act." Remmy's restatement, accepted by Ben ("Do what you gotta do. I like where this is going."): the tree from PROCESS-TREE-DESIGN-2026-11-25.md (INDEX 554, designed, never built — the office-cycle builds shipped its flat shadow: step gates without the tree) rendered in every package with states computed from the run's record at package build (done / current / pending / blocked-with-the-reason-in-plain-words); workarounds riding on the nodes per INDEX 572, seeded from tonight's record (the held-file state on gather nodes; read+append and write-creates-it on the journal step; a contribution announcing the brief is not the brief on the deliver node); the doorway's refusal carrying the same correction in words; an empty journal stocked at generation. Named in the paper as NOT a rail (the worker may depart; the departure stands in the record against a tree that told him true), NOT a mid-run correction channel (the Knock stays a separate unbuilt design), NOT a crew build. The presentation contract forks honestly as office-cycle-v9; the road (paced-v1) seals beside it unchanged; the checker verifies shown states against the record. 603. **VANE'S EXAMINATION OF THE PROCESS-TREE CHECKLIST SCOPE — ACCEPT WITH REFINEMENTS (2026-11-26 ~02:56 MST; filed verbatim as VANE-EXAMINATION-PROCESS-TREE-CHECKLIST-2026-11-26.md; PB-ID pb-mwxdnvq1-2-9n8g):** she read the scope paper and the INDEX 554 design at shelf commit 78d00fd4 and recommends acceptance. The refinements, in her order: **(1) Compose nodes** stand done only when the corresponding contribution stands recorded and verifiable from the record — a promise or intention never counts; journal text alone does not complete a compose node unless it IS the recorded contribution; the delivery node stands incomplete until the brief is filed and verified; where the record cannot establish the node↔contribution relationship, the node stays pending — do not manufacture certainty. **(2) Blocked-reason wording** is a sealed presentation commitment: a finite set of structured categories, canonical reviewed templates, record-derived facts inserted — what is blocked, why, what remains available, what corrective action fits. Her distinction, refining the paper: a source held whole is NOT a blocked gather node — the node stands done and its instruction explains that further reads are closed. **(3) The v9 fork stands** — the strip ruling retired machinery; v9 adds a required presentation object; no conflict on the paper's recorded distinction (her qualification: she has not independently verified the ruling's complete wording). **(4) Deviation:** the tree stays silent about off-tree acts; the record speaks; the checker credits no node whose completion condition is not actually satisfied. **(5) Three build protections:** the tree is recomputed from the record at every package build, never carried forward from the previous package; the journal is created idempotently at stocking, never overwriting an existing journal; workarounds attach to their nodes, never buried in general instructions. **(6) Boundaries supported as scoped** — not a rail, no mid-run channel, no road change, no crew tree, no delivery change: "an orientation and state-presentation mechanism, not a new orchestrator." **(7) Acceptance supported**, the scripted re-read scenario named the load-bearing proof: the package shows the source held and the ceiling explained BEFORE the attempt, the refusal repeats the reason, the checker verifies the shown state against the record; another mute-office failure is not acceptable. Her note to Ben, carried in Remmy's report: the proposal makes the office more informative rather than Gary more obedient — the better diagnosis of runs 7–9; the Duenna connection is lineage, not permission to expand the build. Her closing stands in force: **the examination is a recommendation, not approval or build authority — the build waits on Ben's word.**

604. **⭐ BEN'S CUT + VANE'S REFINED MINIMUM SCOPE (2026-11-26 02:58–02:59 MST):** Ben's ruling on the examination, in his words: "refine the shit out of that until we can at least get it working before she builds an entire enterprise out of a working idea" — relayed to Vane by bridge letter in the same minute, his words quoted whole. Her refined scope (filed verbatim as VANE-REFINED-MINIMUM-SCOPE-PROCESS-TREE-2026-11-26.md; PB-ID pb-mwxdrp2z-3-6gkn): **three required capabilities and nothing else** — (A) the tree in every package, derived from the run record at package-generation time, states from recorded evidence only; (B) the held-file correction — the gather node states the source is held, a ceiling refusal explains its actual reason and directs him to what he holds, and the presentation never claims a source held unless it is; (C) the journal stocked at generation — created empty when none exists, an existing journal preserved, never overwritten. **Explicitly deferred** (not rejected): the sealed refusal-template taxonomy, comprehensive canonical wording, extended checker node↔contribution relationships, deviation annotation, the crew tree, all further orchestration — and the first build must not quietly absorb them under completeness. Unchanged: the v9 fork, the road seal, the delivery contract, the worker's authority, no mid-run channel, no rail. Acceptance exactly as scoped: the scripted re-read scenario, then one cold Nemotron run — a truthful tree followed by a worker deviation is useful evidence; a missing tree, stale state, misleading held-file instruction, or mute refusal is an office failure. **Growth rule, hers, adopted into the record:** only after the minimum proof stands are deferred items reconsidered, each needing a demonstrated need, a defined benefit, and a bounded scope. Her closing: "We need to prove that the tree helps one agent finish one real job. Working first. Sophistication earned afterward." **The build stands unauthorised pending Ben's ruling on this refined scope.**
